<meta>This page acts as a bibliographic addendum, intended to be used alongside the first part of Antoinette Bourignon’s "The Call of God" to provide a complete list of her published works.</meta>

One finds at the end of the first
*Part of The Call of God <note>original: "L’ Appel de Dieu"</note>, the Catalogue of the*
*Books of Mademoiselle Bourignon*, to which
one must add the following books,
which together constitute all
her Works.

*The Life of Miss Antoinette Bourignon in 68 sheets <term>sheets: original "feuilles", a measurement of the physical length of a book based on the number of paper sheets used in printing</term>.*
*The Call of God etc., second Part, 17 sheets.*
*The Stones of the New Jerusalem. 24 sheets.*
*The Testimony of Truth, second Part, etc. 35 sheets.*
*The Blindness of Men Today,*
*second Part.*
*The Persecutions of the Just.*
*The Morning Star.*
*Confusion of the Builders of Babel <note>original: "Ouvriers de Babel", literally the workers or laborers of Babel</note>.*
*Wholesome Advice & Instructions, in two Vol-*
*umes.*

--- Page 2 ---
<meta>This page serves as the title page for "The Last Mercy of God," the first part of a major work by the mystic Antoinette Bourignon, printed in 1681. It follows the bibliographic list provided on the previous page.</meta>

### THE
# LAST
## MERCY
### OF GOD.

Which reveals all essential Truths regarding the Matters of the True Christian Faith; indeed, even those which have not yet been understood or comprehended by men, nor even revealed by God, who has reserved them for the last times, in which we now live at present, where all things shall have their accomplishment in a perfect & complete sense.

->Composed by<-
->**ANTHOINETTE BOURIGNON.**<-
->FIRST PART.<-

->THE COAT OF ARMS<-
<margin>OF THE LILY IN FLANDERS.</margin>
<image-desc>A coat of arms featuring a shield with a single large fleur-de-lis, the emblem of the city of Lille.</image-desc>
<margin>OF THE CITY OF LILLE <note>original: "L’Isle," the archaic spelling of the city of Lille in northern France, the author’s birthplace.</note></margin>
<note>The following line is printed upside-down at the base of the emblem.</note>
->ON THE RIVER<-

->AT AMSTERDAM, at *Jean Rieverts* & *Pierre Arents*,<-
->Booksellers, Exchange Street <note>original: "Rue de la Bourse"</note>, 1681.<-
<insert>S. K.</insert>

The page features the coat of arms of Lille, the author's birthplace, and identifies the work's 1681 publication in Amsterdam.</summary>

## The Kindness of God invites you to repentance. <note>This is a partial citation of Romans 2:4. In the French version used by Bourignon, "benignité" suggests a gentle, parental kindness or "goodness" intended to lead a sinner back to the right path.</note>

<meta>This page follows the scriptural epigraph from Romans 2:4. It begins the formal Preface written by Christian de Cort, a priest and Superior of the Oratory in Mechelen, who became the primary disciple and publisher of Antoinette Bourignon's works. This page introduces Bourignon as a divinely inspired figure born in 1616.</meta>

# PREFACE
->By the late<- <note>original: "De feu." This term refers to a deceased person; Christian de Cort died in 1669, shortly before many of these works were finalized for publication.</note>
## Mr. DE CORT,
->Concerning this<-
## TREATISE.

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut drop-cap "P" featuring stylized floral motifs and scrollwork.</image-desc>

*Since Jesus Christ says that one must not put the candle under a bushel or a bed, but rather on the lampstand, so that it may enlighten all who enter,* <note>A reference to the Parable of the Lamp, found in Matthew 5:15 and Luke 8:16.</note> *I cannot prevent myself from placing upon the lampstand* the marvelous Light which seems to me to be the one that must enlighten the whole world: And as throughout all time God has particularly communicated with certain Prophets, or holy souls, to whom He has made known His secrets and the designs He had for mankind, so that by means of His revelations they might be maintained in their duties; and because the Mercy of God is not limited to certain times, but instead is always manifesting itself toward mankind; it makes itself known once again in our miserable time, filled with miseries and horrible darkness. For in the year 1616, He brought into the world in the city of LILLE in Flanders a creature <note>The word "creature" was a standard 17th-century term for a "created being," often used to emphasize a person's humility and dependence on God.</note> with whom He has always had particular communications ever since, and to whom He has revealed great secrets: And it seems that He wishes to use her to exercise His LAST MERCY,

<vocab>Mr. De Cort, Jesus Christ, bushel, lampstand, Prophets, holy souls, Mercy of God, Lille, Flanders, 1616, Antoinette Bourignon</vocab>

<summary>This preface, written by the late Christian de Cort, introduces Antoinette Bourignon as a divinely chosen vessel born in 1616. He frames her revelations as a "marvelous light" sent by God to guide humanity during a dark and miserable era.</summary>
<keywords>Christian de Cort, Antoinette Bourignon, Lille, Flanders, divine revelation, 17th-century mysticism, Last Mercy, prophecy</keywords>

--- Page 5 ---
<meta>This page continues the narrative of Antoinette Bourignon's divine mission, specifically her 1637 meeting with the Archbishop of Cambrai and the opposition she faced from established religious orders. It picks up from the previous page's description of the "great secrets" revealed to her.</meta>

# PREFACE

& to revive upon the earth his *Gospel Spirit*, <note>original: "Esprit Evangelique." This refers to a return to the simple, lived faith of the early Apostles as described in the New Testament.</note> by drawing men away from their errors to make them recognize the Solid Truth, & to heal them of their blindness.

And to discover the cause of the beginning of the wars & misfortunes that occur in the World, she traveled to the city of MONS in the year 1637 to see Monseigneur *François van der Burcht*, then Archbishop of Cambrai, to whom she declared on behalf of God, *That the abomination of the Ecclesiastics* <note>The secular clergy, such as priests and bishops.</note> *& Religious* <note>Members of religious orders, such as monks, friars, or nuns.</note> *was the cause of God's wrath. That they had abandoned the Spirit of Christianity, & introduced the Pharisaic Spirit:* <note>A reference to the Pharisees of the Bible; implying a focus on outward ritual and legalism rather than inner devotion.</note> *That God would take severe vengeance if they would not surrender themselves, & reclaim the Gospel Spirit*. This she offered to put into practice with a certain number of persons who wished to follow her in this same Gospel Spirit; offering that, in case she were admitted to begin, & should universal peace not follow, she should be placed in a narrow prison, & put to death.

This proposal being examined by the said Archbishop with his Council of Theologians & enlightened persons, was greatly admired, they acknowledging this design as being raised up by the Holy Spirit: for which reason, the said Archbishop permitted her to settle in his Diocese, in order to see put into practice what she had put forward as a proposal. This she has not been able to effect until now: because certain Congregations calling themselves Religious had so much influence & power over the Mind of the said Archbishop, that they made him desist from the secon- <note>The text cuts off here at the end of the page; the catchword indicates the next word is "der," likely "seconde" continuing onto the next page.</note>

<summary>This page recounts Antoinette Bourignon’s 1637 meeting with the Archbishop of Cambrai, where she claimed the world's wars were caused by the corruption of the clergy. While the Archbishop initially approved her plan to revive the "Gospel Spirit," he was eventually pressured by religious orders to withdraw his support.</summary>
<keywords>Antoinette Bourignon, François van der Burcht, Archbishop of Cambrai, Gospel Spirit, Pharisaic Spirit, church corruption, religious orders, Mons, 17th-century mysticism</keywords>

--- Page 6 ---
<meta>This page continues the narrative of Antoinette Bourignon's life, picking up from the end of the previous page where the Archbishop of Cambrai intended to support (se-conder) her mission. This section describes her withdrawal into silence due to persecution and her eventual meeting with the author of this preface.</meta>

# P R E F A C E

* 3

to support and protect her, <note>original: "seconder & la proteger." This completes the sentence from the previous page.</note> filling her mind with doubts, fears, and human considerations. This forced this creature to withdraw into solitude and keep silence: as also because many Priests, Churchmen, and Members of Religious Orders persecuted her furiously, saying that she brought discredit to the Ecclesiastical Estate. <term>Ecclesiastical Estate: The institutional Church, including its hierarchy, laws, and social standing.</term>

To which she did not wish to contest: waiting for God to take up her cause: saying for her only defense:
> *Those who do not wish to believe, will perhaps taste death in a short time:*
applying herself continually to listening to the secret conversations of God, without declaring them further to anyone, until the beginning of the year 1662. Then she found herself horribly persecuted by the people of her Country: so that she was constrained to withdraw into other foreign lands to avoid the fury of her enemies.

This gave me the occasion to meet her, and to discover the truth of what I had for a long time previously only heard said by virtuous persons, worthy of Faith.

I saw with my eyes and heard with my ears admirable things, surpassing all human capacity. This soul revealed to me the most secret thoughts of my heart, and told me several things hidden and unknown to men, which I have since experienced to be true: She predicted several things to me long before they happened, and when there was nothing seemingly likely in appearance, on various occasions, revealing that she truly had the spirit of Prophecy and communicated with God. All the tru-

<meta>catchword: -ths (original: "tus" from "verités")</meta>

<summary>This page describes how Antoinette Bourignon was forced into a period of silence and exile due to fierce opposition from the clergy before meeting the author, who testifies to her prophetic gifts.</summary>

--- Page 7 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's account of his meeting with Antoinette Bourignon. The previous page ended mid-word; the letters "tus" at the start of this page complete the word "vertues" (virtues), referring to the reports the author had heard about Bourignon’s character.</meta>

-># P R E F A C E<-

virtues that had been reported to me about her were nothing compared to those I noticed myself while associating with her. Her life was supernatural, resembling a continual miracle. I was at first a little stunned in my mind by the marvelous things she told me; but having experienced them both in myself and in others, I was forced to surrender, admiring the wonders of God.

I have written a large volume of the **Conversations** <term>Conferences: Formal spiritual discussions or dialogues intended for instruction.</term> I had with her, through which I discovered more science and Doctrine than I could ever learn among all the Universities in the world. Her style was entirely simple and natural, accompanied by great **Wisdom** <term>Sapience: A term for deep, often divine, wisdom that transcends mere human knowledge.</term> and profound knowledge, both Divine and human. Nothing was ever heard like the liveliness and efficacy of her words: her reasons were firm, and her terms convincing: always consistent with herself. From this it appears clearly enough that everything she puts forward is drawn from the Wisdom of the **Holy Spirit**.

I would very much wish that the whole world saw and heard everything that I have seen and heard from this enlightened soul. But I am not yet permitted to say it; rather, I am permitted to bring to light this **Treatise** that she placed in my hands, which seems to me sufficient to convert all men of good will. It is full of Wisdom, Doctrine, Truths, Prophecy, and wonders which have until now been hidden from all human minds.

It is entitled, **THE LAST MERCY OF GOD**: because this soul assures us that we have arrived at the final times where all things

<summary>The author describes his personal awe at Antoinette Bourignon's supernatural wisdom and character, claiming her insights surpass university education and announcing the publication of her treatise, "The Last Mercy of God."</summary>

<keywords>Antoinette Bourignon, divine wisdom, Holy Spirit, mysticism, spiritual autobiography, prophecy, end times</keywords>

--- Page 8 ---
<meta>This page continues the introduction to the "Last Mercy of God." The previous page ended with "final tim...", which is completed here as "final times."</meta>

-># P R E F A C E<-

times will have their full accomplishment; and that all kinds of wickedness will soon be removed from the earth and the Heavens: And that it is also the final summons <note>original: "semonce" — a formal warning or call to repent.</note> that God will make to men to convert them: with so many other unheard-of wonders, capable of ravishing the hearts of all those who seek to work out their salvation: believing that no one could obtain it except those who receive this *Last Mercy of God*, which opens eyes through such clear lights born in the midst of the universal **Darkness** <term>Tenebres: A theological term for spiritual blindness or the state of the world before divine intervention.</term>, in which no one can remain after the reading of this Treatise except those who take pleasure in their blindness, and have no will to leave it to receive the Light of the Truth, which no one can resist except those who are adherents of the Devil, the Father of Lies: For those who resist this Truth, resist God, and cannot be saved: But this *Last Mercy* is sent to those who wish to receive and follow it.

God has fulfilled in this what is said in *Isaiah* (ch. 29), *That Wisdom shall perish from the wise, and understanding shall be hidden from the prudent.* Because all human wisdom must be destroyed and all prudence abolished by the **Wisdom** <term>Sapience: From the Latin 'sapientia,' referring to a deep, infused spiritual wisdom that exceeds human study.</term> of the Holy Spirit, who through the organ <note>Referring to Antoinette Bourignon as the human instrument or "vessel" for the Spirit.</note> of a person who, having never studied and learned nothing by human means, nevertheless knows more than all the men of the world together have ever known. This verifies the word of *Jesus Christ (Matth. 11.)*, that *God hides his secrets from the wise and the prudent, and that he reveals them to the simple and little ones*: as also what St. Paul says, (1. Cor. 1.)

<meta>This page continues the preface's argument that God uses the humble to humble the proud, specifically identifying the author (Antoinette Bourignon) as a "weak girl" who possesses divine knowledge. It introduces her apocalyptic warnings about the reign of the Antichrist and the impending General Judgment.</meta>

that God has chosen the foolish things of this world to confound the Wise; and God has chosen the weak things of this world to confound the strong; and God has chosen the vile things of this world, and the despised, and those that are not, in order to abolish those that are <note>A paraphrase of 1 Corinthians 1:27-28.</note>. For nothing seems weaker than a girl in the **sciences** <term>Sciences: In this 17th-century context, this refers to formal academic study and systematic theology rather than modern natural sciences.</term>, who nevertheless knows the highest mysteries of all kinds of divine sciences; so that everyone will be able to judge by her present *Treatise on the Last Mercy*, which is given by God for the conversion of many, who will see the light born in the **Darkness** <term>Tenebres: Spiritual blindness or a world devoid of divine grace.</term> like a bright sun at its noon, causing the shadows of death to dissipate for all who receive it; and it will also condemn all those who reject this light: since Jesus Christ says, (*John 3*) *This is the condemnation: that the Light has come into the world, and men loved darkness more than the Light.* This is the final condemnation which accompanies this *Last Mercy*: because the end has come, and the world is judged.

These are the words of this Divine Soul <note>The author, Antoinette Bourignon, who claimed to be a direct vessel for the Holy Spirit.</note>, who speaks through the Spirit of God, and says;
> That we have arrived in the Reign of the Antichrist: That the World is judged by the General Judgment: That the sentence is irrevocable: That it is beginning to be put into execution: That wars are the beginnings of **sorrows** <note>original: "douleurs" — a reference to the "birth pangs" of the apocalypse mentioned in the Gospels.</note>: That plague, famine, and other signs will follow as the forerunners of the Coming of Jesus Christ in glory, which will be the last day, at which time there will be the consummation of all evils, and will also be the beginning of the Reign of Jesus Christ with the

--- Page 10 ---
<meta>This page continues the list of prophetic claims made by Antoinette Bourignon, expanding on her assertion that the world has entered the end times. It emphasizes that her insights into the Book of Revelation surpass even those of the original Apostles.</meta>

# P R E F A C E

* 5

<meta>catchword: lution</meta>

She also says that no one had yet understood the Holy Scriptures in their perfect sense—not even the Apostles; and that nothing within them was yet fulfilled except for the death of Jesus Christ. However, she claims that God would give, in this present time, a perfect understanding of all things: That the Apocalypse of St. John <note>The Book of Revelation.</note> encompasses all the aforementioned Scriptures since the beginning of the world, and that everything within it will be fulfilled. She asserts that it contains all the blessings and all the misfortunes that must soon happen to all men.

She says openly: *That Christendom will be the first destroyed by these final plagues, because she has abandoned the practice of the doctrine of Jesus; and that from him to whom much was given, much will be required:* <note>A reference to the Gospel of Luke 12:48.</note> That the Pagans and infidels will be treated more gently than her, because she has abandoned the fountain of living water to draw from broken cisterns of wild waters <note>original: "cîternes crevées des eaux sauvages." This is an allusion to Jeremiah 2:13, where "broken cisterns that cannot hold water" represent a turning away from God.</note>.

She also says that the final plagues will exterminate more than three-quarters of all mankind; that they will be so horrible that nothing similar has ever been seen since the world was made. She claims that men will wither from fear, running here and there as if out of their minds with judgment, wishing for death without being able to find it; but that there will be some small place where the **friends of God** <term>Friends of God: Likely a reference to "Gottesfreunde," a medieval and early modern mystical movement that emphasized an inner, personal relationship with God above outward ritual.</term> will retreat.

She told me, finally, so many details of the wonders of God that I have often been transported outside of myself; and her words have worked such operations in my soul that I have taken the reso-

<summary>In this preface, the author presents Antoinette Bourignon’s claim to have received a divine, "perfect understanding" of the Book of Revelation, warning that a corrupt Christendom will be the first to fall in a coming apocalypse that will destroy most of humanity.</summary>
<keywords>Antoinette Bourignon, Apocalypse, Christendom, Judgment, Holy Scriptures, Prophecy, Friends of God</keywords>

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's personal reaction to Antoinette Bourignon’s prophecies. The previous page ended with the beginning of the word "resolution."</meta>

# P R E F A C E

* 5

<meta>catchword: &</meta>

[reso]lution to abandon the World, and to leave every status, office, and benefit, in order to better prepare my soul for the expectation of all these things, holding them to be as true as if God himself had spoken them to me. This is because in the Holy Prophets, of both the Old and the New Testament, I find all the things she has told me—but more obscurely. She speaks so clearly and with such vivid and convincing terms and reasons that everyone can easily judge the time has come when all the Prophecies will be fulfilled; for a young woman possesses a perfect understanding of them. Everything that the other Prophets spoke of through figures and parables, she says so plainly <note>original: "naïvement." In this context, it means with natural simplicity and directness rather than gullibility.</note> that everyone can understand it, the simple as well as the wise, and the ignorant as well as the learned. In short, it is like the mirror of the true light, or the radiance of the Sun among the stars.

As for me, I judge her to be the woman spoken of in the 12th chapter of the Apocalypse <note>The Book of Revelation 12:1 describes a "woman clothed with the sun, with the moon under her feet, and on her head a crown of twelve stars."</note>. This is something she never wished to admit to me; but by the marks of the qualities and conditions that such a woman must possess, I can make a well-supported conjecture. Having gained a precise knowledge of her, and having informed myself exactly through several good people worthy of faith, I have learned that since her childhood she has been possessed by the Holy Spirit. She has trodden underfoot all riches, pleasures, and honors as transitory things, symbolized by *the Moon*; she has also always been seated in the Truth as if *in a Sun*; and her understanding is filled with divine and eternal lights, symbolized by *the Crown of twelve stars*.

<summary>The author describes his decision to renounce worldly life to follow Antoinette Bourignon's teachings, identifying her as the prophetic "Woman clothed with the Sun" from the Book of Revelation.</summary>
<keywords>Antoinette Bourignon, Revelation 12, end times, prophecy, renunciation, Holy Spirit, biblical interpretation</keywords>

--- Page 12 ---
<meta>This page continues the editor’s interpretation of Antoinette Bourignon as the "Woman of the Apocalypse" from Revelation 12. The previous page ended describing her as "seated in the T[hrone]" or perhaps "the Truth," and this page picks up with the description of her spiritual struggle against the Devil.</meta>

# P R E F A C E

* 6

<meta>catchword: conten-</meta>

—and that the Devil has always pursued and dogged her to prevent her designs, and to ensure that the work God wishes to perform through her never comes to be born; although he has never had any power over her person. I have also seen her often and for long periods *in pains like a woman in labor* <note>A reference to Revelation 12:2, "And she being with child cried, travailing in birth, and pained to be delivered."</note>, desiring to give birth to souls for God—not through effeminate, inconstant, or weak desires or wills, but through those that are masculine <note>In the 17th century, "masculine" was often used to describe spiritual strength, vigor, and constancy, as opposed to "effeminate" weakness.</note>, solid, and effective. She cries out continually to see souls brought into the light of the Spirit and the Evangelical doctrine <term>Evangelical doctrine: The "Spirit of the Gospel," focused on inner rebirth rather than outward ritual.</term>, and solid virtue. Meanwhile, the Devil is always watching continually in all kinds of encounters to try to swallow up the pious designs she has to produce this Evangelical Spirit, even though he has never had any power over her person, as I have said.

Be that as it may, it will suffice for everyone to believe the things that can convert them to God, since this is the sole purpose of the Editor, who wishes for everyone the same benefits of light and grace that he himself has received through the knowledge of this soul guided by God. She cannot know what she declares unless the Holy Spirit reveals it to her, for she has no desire to learn anything from men; she flees their conversation, making use of no Directors <note>Spiritual directors: professional clergy who guided the spiritual lives of the faithful. Bourignon claimed her only guide was God.</note>, nor the reading of books, saying that all these things serve as great hindrances to her.

I never saw her affect, pretend, desire, or seek anything of the things of this life, nor any human creature, however great, powerful, or perfect they might be; nor take any

<summary>The editor describes Antoinette Bourignon’s spiritual labor to "give birth" to new souls for God while being pursued by the Devil, emphasizing her direct inspiration from the Holy Spirit without human guidance.</summary>
<keywords>Antoinette Bourignon, Revelation 12, spiritual labor, Holy Spirit, spiritual directors, Devil, Evangelical Spirit, mysticism</keywords>

--- Page 13 ---
<meta>This page continues the editor’s preface, moving from his personal testimony of the subject’s spiritual labor and struggles with the Devil to his formal resolution to leave his worldly life to follow her teachings.</meta>

# P R E F A C E

<meta>catchword: &</meta>

—resolution to abandon the World, and leave all status, offices, and church positions <term>benefices: ecclesiastical offices that provided a guaranteed income to the holder</term>, in order to better prepare my soul for the expectation of all these things, holding them to be as true as if God Himself had spoken them to me. This is because in the Holy Prophets, both of the Old and New Testaments, I find all the things she told me, but more obscurely; for she speaks so clearly and with such lively and convincing terms and reasons, that everyone can well judge that the time has come when all the Prophecies will be fulfilled. This is evident since a girl has a perfect understanding of them; and all that the other Prophets said through figures and parables, she says so plainly <note>original: "naïvement," which in the 17th century meant naturally, directly, and without artifice</note> that everyone can understand, the simple as well as the wise, and the ignorant as well as the learned. In short, she is like the mirror of the true light, or the star of the Sun among the stars.

For my part, I judge her to be the woman spoken of in the 12th chapter of the Apocalypse <note>The Book of Revelation 12:1: "A woman clothed with the sun, and the moon under her feet, and upon her head a crown of twelve stars."</note>. She has never been willing to admit this to me; but by the marks of the qualities and conditions that this woman must possess, I can make a well-supported conjecture. Having gained a precise knowledge of her, and having informed myself exactly from many people of good standing who are worthy of faith, I have learned that from her Infancy she has been possessed by the Holy Spirit, having *trampled underfoot* all riches, pleasures, and honors as transitory things, symbolized by *the Moon*. She has also always been seated in the Truth *as in a Sun*, and her understanding is filled with divine and eternal lights, symbolized by *the Crown of twelve stars*.

<summary>The editor declares his decision to renounce worldly status to follow Antoinette Bourignon, arguing that her clear explanations of scripture prove the end times are near and identifying her as the "Woman of the Apocalypse."</summary>
<keywords>Antoinette Bourignon, Revelation 12, prophecy, worldliness, Holy Spirit, spiritual clarity, Apocalypse</keywords>

--- Page 14 ---
<meta>This page continues the editor’s preface, completing the description of the subject (Antoinette Bourignon) as the symbolic woman of the Apocalypse. It contrasts her spiritual labor with the opposition of the Devil and emphasizes her lack of reliance on human teaching or spiritual directors.</meta>

# P R E F A C E

<meta>catchword: conten-</meta>

—and that the Devil has always *pursued* and dogged her to prevent her designs, and to ensure that the work God wishes to accomplish through her is never born; although he has never had any power over her person. I have also seen her often and for a long time *in pains like a woman in childbirth* <note>A continuation of the imagery from Revelation 12:2: "And she being with child cried, travailing in birth, and pained to be delivered."</note>, desiring to give birth to souls for God—not through effeminate, inconstant, or weak desires, but *masculine*, solid, and effective ones <note>In 17th-century spiritual writing, "masculine" (mâles) was often used to describe virtues that were robust, rational, and firm, as opposed to purely emotional or "weak" piety</note>. She cries out continually to see souls brought into the light of the Spirit, the Evangelical doctrine, and solid virtue; while the Devil is always watching, constantly, in all types of encounters, to try to swallow up the pious designs she has to produce this Evangelical Spirit <note>original: "l'Esprit Evangelique," referring to the pure, original spirit of the Gospel</note>—though he has never had any power over her person, as I have said.

Whatever the case may be, it will suffice for everyone to believe the things that can convert them to God, since this is the sole purpose of the Editor <note>Likely Pierre Poiret, the philosopher who became Bourignon's primary editor and disciple</note>, who wishes for everyone the same benefits of light and grace that he himself has received through the knowledge of this soul guided by God. She could not know what she declares unless the Holy Spirit revealed it to her; for she has no desire to learn anything from men and avoids their conversation, making use of no <term>Directors: spiritual advisors, usually priests, who provide personal guidance on the soul's progress</term>, nor the reading of books, saying that all these things serve as great hindrances to her.

I never saw her affect, pretend, desire, or seek anything from the things of this life, nor any human creature, however great, powerful, or perfect they might be; nor take any

<summary>The editor describes the subject's spiritual struggles as a form of "labor" to birth new souls for God, despite constant demonic opposition. He emphasizes that her wisdom comes directly from the Holy Spirit rather than from human teachers, books, or spiritual directors.</summary>
<keywords>Antoinette Bourignon, spiritual labor, Revelation 12, Devil, Holy Spirit, spiritual directors, divine inspiration, Pierre Poiret</keywords>

# P R E F A C E

->P R E F A C E<-

<meta>catchword: des</meta>

—contentment only in divine things; and never sad or dissatisfied in sufferings and adversities: her spirit always tranquil and joyful, without mutation or change, without movements of any vicious passion, having conquered, regulated, and subjected them all in every encounter: her temperament good and agreeable; but severe against evil, and an enemy of injustice; peaceful; charitable; having a mother’s heart toward all kinds of people: sober, chaste, honest, and gracious; possessing the twelve fruits of the Holy Spirit <note>The Vulgate Bible (Galatians 5:22-23) lists twelve fruits of the Spirit: charity, joy, peace, patience, kindness, goodness, long-suffering, mildness, faith, modesty, continence, and chastity.</note>, with his gifts, and the eight beatitudes <note>The blessings recounted by Jesus in the Sermon on the Mount (Matthew 5:3-12).</note>.

All these things are to me certain testimonies that the World and the Devil can have no place in her soul: Also, she speaks of things that men or Devils cannot know. For the Devils do not know the secrets and designs of God; and men have only limited knowledge; whereas this soul penetrates the works of God without limits and into infinity.

I have often persuaded her to be willing to communicate with men: which she did not wish to do, saying that she would lose her time there without any profit to others: That the men of today are in such great darkness, that those of Egypt <note>A reference to the biblical Plague of Darkness (Exodus 10:21), used here as a metaphor for spiritual blindness.</note> were only a foreshadowing <note>original: "figure," a theological term for a symbolic precursor</note> of this present time: That they resist the truth which rebukes them, and no longer wish to receive it; and even the most powerful prevent it from coming to light: That the good dare not receive it; and that the wicked pursue and persecute it, just as the Synagogue <note>Used here in a historical-polemical sense to refer to the religious establishment of Jerusalem that opposed Jesus.</note> did in the past to Jesus Christ: And rather than make...

This refers to Bourignon’s prophetic message and her book *The Light of the World*, which she presented as God's final call to a corrupt humanity.</note>, which she wrote with her own hand so that men might return to the knowledge of God and of themselves; that they might recognize the Love He has shown them, creating them so that they might love Him: that they might consider their Origin, and the dignity of the State in which God created them; so that they might do all their duty to recover it, without lingering any longer in the dangerous state into which they have fallen through blindness and ignorance: that they might know their nothingness <note>In 17th-century mysticism, "nothingness" (néant) refers to the humble recognition that the human creature has no power or existence independent of God.</note>, in order to attribute to God all that they are and can be; and that they might finally see the miserable state in which we live at present, so that they may emerge from it in this world to go at death to enjoy God for all eternity. # FOREWORD

->*On the Treatise*<-

## ON THE LAST
## Mercy of God.

->MY CHRISTIAN BROTHER,<-

I am moved by a deep regret to see the whole world perish: and the greatest urgency of heart that grips me is to see it indifferent to its own misfortune. All men <margin>Ps. 14 v. 2, 3.</margin> are blind, and no one recognizes their own blindness. Most believe themselves to be clear-sighted <margin>John 9. v. 41.</margin> and therefore do not seek a remedy for their blindness; rather, they all perish imperceptibly through a lethargy of the soul contracted from one another; each being affected by a spiritual slumber regarding the salvation of their soul; and no one sees where they are walking in these dangerous times; no more than did all <margin>Exod. 10. v. 33.</margin> the Egyptians during the profound darkness of Egypt, which was but a symbol <note>original: "figure." In 17th-century theology, a "figure" is a historical event in the Old Testament that foreshadows a spiritual reality.</note> of the inner darkness of our present time, where everyone runs toward perdition without noticing it, nor fearing the dangers of their damnation. People complain more of their worldly or physical misfortunes than of those which are eternal and concern the salvation of souls.

---

<term>Foreword</term> <note>original: "Avant-propos." An introductory section setting the stage for the book's themes.</note>
<term>Mercy of God</term> <note>original: "Misericorde de Dieu." A central theological concept referring to God's compassion and forgiveness toward sinners.</note>
<term>Salvation of the soul</term> <note>original: "salut de l'ame." The deliverance from sin and its consequences.</note>
<term>Darkness of Egypt</term> <note>original: "tenebres d'Egypte." A reference to the ninth plague of Egypt (Exodus 10), used here as a metaphor for spiritual ignorance.</note>
<term>Damnation</term> <note>original: "damnation." The state of eternal separation from God.</note>

<summary>In this opening foreword, the author expresses her grief over the spiritual blindness of humanity, comparing the current state of the world to the biblical plague of darkness in Egypt. She warns that people are more concerned with physical hardships than the eternal danger facing their souls.</summary>
<keywords>spiritual blindness, salvation, mercy of God, darkness of Egypt, spiritual lethargy, 17th-century theology, Antoinette Bourignon</keywords>

# FOREWORD

2. This stems from the fact that no one knows God anymore in His attributes <note>original: "qualités"</note>, nor under what conditions all His works are accompanied. And this ignorance means that man also does not know himself, nor does he understand the matter from which he was made, nor the purpose <note>original: "fin" — in this context, the ultimate goal or destiny of humanity.</note> of his creation; and thus, he clings to the earth and to objects perceptible to his base senses; and forgets his dignity, and the eternal goods that ought to come to him. It is truly lamentable to see a divine soul feeding upon human things, and an eternal creature seeking only temporal things.

3. However, everyone is now caught in this, and no one knows the eternal truth anymore. We are up to our necks in waves of errors and lies; which has produced a deluge of sins and evils that seem irremediable, unless with a contrite heart we address ourselves to God, crying out with perfect contrition, MERCY! <note>original: "MISERICORDE"</note>; and saying with St. Peter: <margin>(d) Matt. 8. v. 25.</margin> *(d) Save us, Lord, for we are all perishing:* <note>The author translates the Latin Vulgate phrase "Domine, salva nos, perimus" into French; here rendered in English.</note> to see if by chance we might once again obtain His mercy, through which we might save our souls. For I cannot perceive by what means a single soul could be saved in the way people live now: because the Law of God is smothered by the sciences of men; and the Gospel Doctrine <term>Gospel Doctrine</term> <note>original: "Doctrine Evangelique." The core teachings of the New Testament.</note> is nullified by their practices; and God is altogether unrecognized because of the authority that men have taken over one another: so that they make themselves more feared and esteemed than one fears

We see all the States <term>States</term> <note>original: "Estats" — referring to the social orders or estates of society (clergy, nobility, and commoners).</note> overturned, the people unruly and dissolute, <margin>(e) Hosea 4:1</margin> *(e)* without faith, without loyalty, and without fear of God: malice dominates, and innocence is oppressed: the guilty are supported and the righteous are abandoned: charity is dead even among those who claim to be good people. Instead, we occupy ourselves with covering up our misfortunes; and with fabricated <note>original: "controuvés" — meaning forged, invented, or false.</note> talk and reasoning, we charm our senses so as not to perceive the scourges <term>scourges</term> <note>original: "fleaux" — divine punishments or calamities sent as warnings.</note> hanging over our heads. These are the voices of Sirens <note>A reference to the mythological creatures whose beautiful singing lured sailors to their doom.</note> predicting the shipwreck; and we take pleasure in their songs: <margin>(g) 1 Corinthians 10:11</margin> *(g)* *The end times have come, and their end is at the door.* *The world is judged*, and <margin>(h) Matthew 24:6</margin> *(h)* *the final scourges have begun.*

7. People imagine (quite wrongly) that the Judg-
ment will take place in an instant, or in a few days.
This cannot be true: for God never sur-
prises anyone on His part. He always gives
several warnings; and even when He punishes <gloss>(p)</gloss> it is always with order and meas-
ure, giving time until the very end for the sin-
ner <gloss>(q)</gloss> to be converted to Him. If He acts this way in
regard to all His individual works, how
would He not do so in regard to His General
Judgment? Would He have more goodness and mer-
cy for an individual sinner than He will have
for the entire universal world? That cannot be.
If He gives a sinner internal admonitions <note>original: "admonitions" — serious moral warnings or rebukes from God or one's conscience.</note>, 
warnings, and several opportunities to 
be converted to Him: sometimes through prosperity, 
to draw them by love; at other times through adversity, 
to make them reflect upon themselves: and so many other 
means that everyone can experience within them-
selves, and judge if God has ever caught us by surprise; 
and if He does not usually even send at the end 
a sickness to predict death before it ar-
rives. This being so, as it is.

# FOREWORD.

runners having appeared, and that all men have revolted against God: yet no one wishes to wait for this Judgment in its own time: nevertheless it must come once: therefore it will come like a thief; at a time when men do not wish to wait for it, and when they will not expect it at all. This is seen clearly enough now, where one feels in various places that the plagues have begun, and almost everyone groans under the burden of miseries; while hearts remain hardened, refusing to believe that the time has come, which Jesus Christ predicted must be <margin>(s) Luke 7. v. 26. <note>The author likely intended to cite Luke 17:26, which describes the days of Noah; Luke 7:26 refers to John the Baptist.</note></margin> *as in the days of Noah, when they ate, drank, and were married, until all were submerged by the waters of the flood.*

9. This sign of unbelief <term>unbelief</term> <note>original: "incredulité" — here meaning a stubborn refusal to recognize divine warnings or the spiritual significance of current events.</note> is the most certain <margin>(t) Luke 18. v. 8. 1 Thess. 5. v. 3.</margin> of the signs forerunning the last day. For when it was preached in times past that the Judgment had then arrived, many believed, and were converted to God through penance. Their belief in that very thing, even though it was not then true, made them return to God and work out their salvation: and now that it is so true that one sees it with one's own eyes, and can truly understand it with one's intellect, no one wants to believe it! This sign of unbelief confirms that the Judgment is truly here more than any other signs. This happens because people reflect more upon the discourses of men than upon the signs predicted by Jesus Christ and so many holy Prophets, who have so plainly <note>original: "naïvement" — in 17th-century French, this meant "plainly," "naturally," or "faithfully," rather than "naively" in the modern sense.</note> depicted the way of life of the men of today that no one should doubt it: yet no one believes it.

<meta>This page continues the "Foreword." The previous page established that the current era's skepticism is the surest sign of the approaching end, as people ignore the warnings predicted by Christ.</meta>

### Last plagues. General Judgment.
<sig>A 4</sig>

10. It seems that one wishes to be wicked simply because God is good. If he sends his plagues slowly in order to give men the time to convert, it is a great goodness and mercy he shows them. If he sent them all at once, everyone would be caught in a bad state; or if he put the universal sentence into execution so suddenly, no one would have the time or opportunity to perform penance <term>penance</term> <note>original: "penitence" — in this context, it refers to the spiritual discipline of repentance and making amends for sins.</note>; all would die in their sins. But the excess of God’s goodness wishes to perform *this last mercy*, sending his plagues to the world in order and in measure, so that no one is caught by surprise except those who wish to let themselves be surprised. For the wars that we see at present are <gloss>(u)</gloss> the beginning of the sickness which presages our certain death at the end of it. **It is incurable and mortal regarding our body;** but it is not mortal for our soul, which can still be healed by these very plagues. # ->PREFACE<-

God, having at the beginning of our final age <note>The "final age" or "last times" refers to the era of the Christian dispensation following the birth of Christ.</note> sent His word to men through the body of Jesus Christ, gave it the power **to bind and to loose**, <term>to bind and to loose</term> <note>original: "lier & délier" — a biblical reference to Matthew 16:19, signifying the authority to declare what is forbidden or permitted, or to forgive sins.</note> with the promise that He will approve all that it does; because of the unity that exists between God and His Word, which is so close that they are one and the same thing. And so, what it does on earth will surely be done in heaven: because it is the same will of God acting through Jesus Christ. In the same way, the wills of those souls who possess the doctrine of Jesus Christ will forever be united to the will of God; and He will approve and perform what they do for all eternity. But to believe that God will perform and endorse the things that men do here on earth out of their own impulses or inclinations is a great deception.

25. When men tell us the Law of God and the **Gospel Doctrine**, they must be believed and followed; not because they say it, but because it is God who tells it to us through their ministry. But when they act according to their own senses, and when they make Laws, Decrees, and Ordinances according to human conceptions (even if they were assembled in great numbers), they can err and fail. For *(s)* *all men are liars*, <note>original: "tous hommes ſont menteurs" — a quote from Psalm 116:11.</note> being capable of deceiving and being deceived. There can be no other Truth, Goodness, Justice, and Power than those which are in God; and men can possess none of these qualities, in whatever state or condition they may be, unless they participate in them from God Himself.

---

->### Source of Good and Evil.<-
B 2
<meta>catchword: ve-</meta>

falsehood, injustice, malice, powerlessness, and mutability. For one contrary always drives out its opposite; no evil can ever remain within a perfect good, or it immediately ceases to be good: likewise, no good can ever be found within perfect evil without it ceasing to be evil.

30. Therefore it must be concluded that all things in the world are assuredly either good or evil; because by necessity everything good we do or say has its source, or takes its origin, from God, who is all good: for from man comes all evil. *God and His Love unrecognized through studies.*

...all good and all-powerful; since all these qualities are found in all His works. This knowledge, although it might be considered merely pagan <note>The author refers to "natural theology"—the idea that one can know God simply by observing the natural world, a concept Paul the Apostle discusses in the cited verse.</note>, would nevertheless bring more advantage to the salvation of souls <margin>(p) Romans 1:21</margin> than all the studies and doctrines of men have done. For one could not know the qualities of the works of God without loving Him and abandoning oneself to Him: but the knowledge that men give us of God through their studies withdraws us from Him more than it brings us near; and all their doctrines seem rather to aim at flattering and ruining us, than at leading us to salvation.

37. For they bring so many <term>glosses<note>Interpretive comments or explanations added to a text, often criticized by mystics for obscuring the simple, direct meaning of scripture.</note> and explanations of the commandments of God and the <term>Gospel Law<note>The "Evangelical Law" or "Gospel Law" refers to the spiritual requirements and teachings of Christ found in the New Testament.</note>, that they strip away all their life and substance. How many explanations are there on the Commandment to LOVE GOD? One says that loving Him means not offending Him; another says that it is to love Him when one keeps His Commandments; another says that it is impossible to keep this very specific commandment that God gave to man from the beginning of the world. They would like to avoid it through subtleties of the mind, and offer reasons to teach indirectly that one is not obliged to keep it; and though their arguments may be quite absurd, many people are still found who follow them; because they find that through these doctrines their <term>sensualities<note>In this context, "sensualities" refers to the indulgence of physical desires and worldly comforts over spiritual devotion.</note> are supported, their vices authorized, and their frailties flattered and excused.

38. This is no small misfortune for those who perish because of these flatterers and liars. For it cannot be true that to love God is simply to stop offending Him <note>The author is refuting the idea that "love" is merely a lack of sin—a "negative" definition of love that he finds spiritually hollow.</note>, since we do not love everyone whom we do not offend. For those we offend, we hate, and they are our enemies. Now if we offend God, we do not merely stop loving Him; we also consider Him our enemy and one whom we hate; and hating is something other than ceasing to offend: for we stop hating many people whom we nevertheless do not offend at all. And he who says that loving God is simply keeping His commandments is no less far from the truth than the first: because we do not love everyone whose commandments we keep; rather, we sometimes hate them. When we have some King, Judge, or Governor who commands things that are disagreeable or disadvantageous to us, we keep [obey] them: but this is no testimony that we love the one who gave these laws; rather, it is an obligation which we cannot escape without punishment <note>The author draws a sharp distinction between legalistic obedience—following rules to avoid the "punishment" of a judge—and the spiritual love God actually requires.</note>.

41. Can more precise or clearer terms be found than to tell a man that he must love God? Now, this word *to love* is understood and practiced by every person in the world. What gloss or explanation <note>A "gloss" is an explanatory note or interpretation added to a text, often criticized by this author as over-complicating simple truths.</note> could one want for it? We also know what properties this <term>LOVE: the author defines this as a natural drive toward seeking and honoring the beloved.</term> possesses: it seeks, it honors, it esteems, it follows, and conforms itself as much as possible to the object it loves. This is what love unfailingly produces. There is no need for <term>Doctors: high-ranking academic theologians.</term> nor <term>Casuists: specialists who resolve moral dilemmas through complex logic, often accused of finding excuses for sin.</term> to explain the properties of *Love*: because every individual carries them naturally within themselves, and can understand what God asks of us when He *says that we should love Him*.

For he says regarding our words, <margin>(h) Matthew 5:37</margin> that we must say "yes" and "no," and whatever is more than this is evil <note>original: "ce qui est de plus est mauvais"</note>; to teach with what sincerity we must deal with our neighbor, and that "yes" and "no" are enough to declare the truth of everything. He says regarding our <term>conversation: our social interactions and way of life</term>, that we must <margin>(i) Luke 14:10</margin> choose the last place and take the lowest position; and that this will bring us honor. He says concerning our <term>meat: food in general, following the archaic French usage of 'viande'</term>, that we must <margin>(l) Luke 10:7</margin> eat what is put before us without being selective in the choice of our food. He also says that <margin>(m) Matthew 6:19–20</margin> we should not amass riches in this world, where moth <note>original: "tigne"</note> and rust spoil everything, and where thieves steal; but that we must amass them for Heaven, where rust spoils nothing and thieves cannot enter. He says that we must not be deceived: <margin>(n) 1 Corinthians 6:9–10; Revelation 21:15</margin> that the <term>lewd: "paillards" in the original, referring to the sexually immoral</term> and drunkards will not enter the Kingdom of Heaven, and <margin>(o) Luke 13:3</margin> that we must <term>do penance: perform acts of repentance</term>; otherwise we will all perish. And along with so many other things, he teaches and admonishes us so precisely in every point that no one can be ignorant of anything concerning the conduct of our life, because he said all this with words that are simply meaningful <note>original: "simplement significatives," suggesting straightforward and plain language</note>, so that everyone might understand them in order to practice them.

# *Human explanations are useless and harmful.*

...we will all perish; nevertheless, everyone presumes their own salvation without repentance <note>original: "penitence"</note>, which they hold in aversion; and painful things are received only with regret; comfort and delights please us, and suffering is hateful.

66. So that instead of following and imitating Jesus Christ as His Disciples, even if we bear His name, we contradict Him by all our works. And despite all this, people believe, through an imaginary presumption, that they are saved. In this, one is greatly mistaken, or allows oneself to be blindly deceived by those who tell us that salvation is certain, contrary to what Jesus Christ predicted for us, *that we will all perish.*

67. I do not know what subtle arguments men might invent to change the words of God, who is, was, and shall be forever the same without any change. If one followed His doctrine even from a distance, one might hope to follow it more closely in the future; but when one does the direct opposite, it is to damn oneself on credit <note>This phrase suggests that by ignoring God's word now, one is essentially "purchasing" their future damnation in advance.</note>. this impartial Judge who needs neither witness, nor Doctors <note>original: "Docteurs," referring to learned theologians or scholars of law rather than medical doctors.</note>, nor Orators to judge our cause. All these arguments, these subtle reasonings, these probable opinions, are all discredited currency <note>original: "monnoyes décriées," a metaphor comparing human excuses to coins that have been withdrawn from circulation or declared worthless by the government.</note> at the Tribunal of this just Judge. Only the upright truth will be admitted and have a place in defending our cause; and if we live in errors and lies, what truth will be able to follow us to death? And to die in a lie is to take sides with the Devil, who is its father. What a misfortune hangs over everyone’s head, while no one raises their head to notice it!

73. I know well, my dear Reader, that this condemnation, so general to all men of our time, will perhaps seem too harsh and too universal to you, when you believe that there are still many good people in the world today, and when you yourself believe you are in the grace of God. But be assured that it is not so before God. You will see this very clearly if you have the time to read the rest of this Treatise <note>original: "Traité," referring to the present book or discourse.</note> with attention. It will show you the purpose for which man was created, the things he must observe, and those which God has reserved for him throughout eternity. You will also learn there to know God in His qualities, which are revealed by the works He has done for man. ## FOREWORD

[Abra]ham, whether he would not want to pardon the world in case you could show him ten more who would do well? He would surely grant it to you, even if it were even fewer: because you would have great difficulty in finding even *one*. <note>A reference to Genesis 18, where Abraham pleads with God to spare the city of Sodom if even ten righteous people can be found there.</note>

74. For my part, I can say with truth that for more than thirty years I have sought a person who was entirely abandoned <term>abandonné: a term in mystical theology referring to "holy indifference" or the total surrender of one's own will to God's</term> to the will of God, without having yet met one until now. I have indeed known many good people who, in comparison to others, were holy personages; however, they did not possess the first condition that God required of all men from the very beginning of their creation—namely, the dependence of their free will upon that of God, in recognition that all things came from Him: being and life, the temporal and the spiritual; that nothing was made without Him, and that everything belongs to Him. I have never, I say, found a single person who conducted themselves in all their works according to this dependence, because no one establishes their origin, nor the essence of the duty they are obliged to have toward their God.

75. One stops only to notice what men do at present; and they follow one another blindly; and they esteem as "good people" those who do less evil than others. But no one perceives that one must be abandoned to God to be able to do good, and that everything which issues from our own will is evil before God. Even if these things were good in the eyes and judgment of men, they are always...

->### Disposition of A. B. and her Readers.<-

...always evil and sins, when they originate from our own will: because it is the source of all evils. This is something no one perceives, even though it is so clear and so true: as you will see in the remainder of this Treatise, which will reveal to you the truth of all things in their source and their origin. <note>The author "A. B." refers to Antoinette Bourignon (1616–1680), a Flemish mystic who claimed to receive direct revelations from God and criticized the established churches of her time.</note>

\* 76. One must not give place to thoughts of disbelief, nor to those feelings that might make my propositions seem harsh at first; rather, one must penetrate them with good judgment, in order to know how to discover whether they are true or not. For otherwise, you will surely perish in the darkness, without wanting to leave it. No one should be an enemy to their own well-being; and no goods are equal to the knowledge of the truth. Therefore, my dear Reader, ensure that neither fear, nor preoccupation, <term>preoccupation: in this context, it refers to preconceived notions or prejudices that prevent one from seeing new truths</term> nor anything you may have learned, heard, or believed until now, holds you back or prevents you from discovering the truth that is so necessary for your salvation <term>salut: the state of being saved from sin and its consequences; eternal life</term>: but with a humble heart, listen to what God will say to you through my pen; and you will be forced to surrender to God, who calls you through the opening of these truths.

77. Never believe that I seek anything other than your conversion to salvation; for if I sought to please men, I would not be of God, and would not tell you such pressing truths; knowing well that they always take pleasure in hearing what redounds to their praise or advantage; and that it is equally unpleasant for them to hear things that make them sad or are reprehensible: because nature <note>The text breaks off here; the catchword "ture" at the bottom of the page indicates that the word "nature" (likely "human nature") continues onto the following page.</note>

<summary>Antoinette Bourignon warns readers that the human will is the source of all evil and urges them to approach her writings with an open, humble mind. she asserts that her harsh truths are proof of her divine mission, as she seeks to save souls rather than win human approval.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the sentence from page 49, where Antoinette Bourignon argues that her harsh words are not a matter of choice but of divine necessity. It completes the thought that seeking to please humans would be a betrayal of the truth.</meta>

-># P R E F A C E.<-

...nature always recoils from what is contrary to it, and wishes to be followed rather than controlled: But I can take no heed of anything except the pure truth <term>verité: in Bourignon's thought, "Truth" is often synonymous with the person of God</term>: since it alone is God <term>Dieu</term>, and I wish to please nothing else.

78. It would be much more pleasant for me if I could say with truth that all the people in the world are on the path to salvation <term>salut: the state of being saved from sin and granted eternal life</term>, and that the majority of them will be saved; rather than to say that all are going to perdition <term>perdition: eternal ruin or loss of the soul</term>. This is something that is repugnant to even the most pious souls. But one must follow the light <term>lumiere divine: the internal spiritual insight granted by God</term> that God gives us, without stopping to consider what will be pleasing or unpleasing to men; I cannot have regard for them without stifling the divine light. If I could see a Church <term>Eglise</term>, a Sect <term>Secte</term>, a Religion <term>Religion</term>, or a profession of persons who were truly abandoned to God <note>abandoned to God: a mystical term meaning the total surrender of one's own will and desires to the will of God</note>, I would rejoice greatly and would publish their praises at every opportunity with contentment, reminding them of the happiness that awaits them for all eternity. But alas! with a sad feeling, I am obliged to declare openly that all men are perishing, that no one knows the truth anymore, and that all have abandoned God.

79. If I were to say otherwise, I would be following lies and deception; and if I conceal the truth, I cooperate in the damnation <term>damnation: the state of being condemned to eternal punishment</term> of those who perish through ignorance <term>ignorance: here, a lack of spiritual knowledge or awareness of God's requirements</term>. It is better to help one's neighbor through suffering than to let them perish through ignorance. Could there really be people so ungrateful as to bear ill will toward those who offer them the truth to enlighten them in such necessary matters?

<summary>Antoinette Bourignon explains that she feels divinely compelled to speak harsh truths about the state of humanity, claiming that everyone has abandoned God and is heading for perdition. She argues that concealing this reality would be a betrayal of God and would contribute to the spiritual ruin of others.</summary>

->### Dispositions of A. B. & of her Readers.###<-
->D 2<-
<meta>catchword: n'y</meta>

...it cannot be human malice; they must be diabolical: because the messenger ought not to be poorly received for bringing unpleasant news, except when he is sent to persons who have lost their judgment. I shall say nothing out of passion or reproach, nor to offend anyone; even less to promote novelties <term>nouveautés: in 17th-century religious contexts, "novelties" referred to new or fashionable doctrines that strayed from ancient, traditional Christian truths</term>. For I am an enemy of all that, and seek neither honor, nor profit, nor the pleasures of anyone in the world; nor do I desire to be believed or followed in my own opinions, for which I have no attachment, having no self-interest in them.

80. I only wish that everyone would look within themselves, and examine the depths of their soul, to see if their conscience does not dictate to them that everything I say is the pure truth, which everyone must follow if they wish to be saved. Those who wish to flatter themselves with false hopes are free to neglect these words, for God constrains no one, and is not obliged to send Angels to speak to us; since the truth in itself is more than the Angels; for it is <gloss>(s)</gloss> God himself. It is therefore up to each person to know if they have the will to follow it or not; since God has given us all free will, which He will never take back: everyone must use it for their salvation, or for their damnation, according to the choice they make. As for me, I have fulfilled my duty once I have told you the pure truth as far as GOD has made it known to me.

81. This is an author <note>original: "autheur" — Bourignon often refers to God as the "Author" of her writings, positioning herself merely as his scribe or channel</note> who must not be censored or controlled; but rather followed and imitated. For if he...

For example: A person will make a solemn vow to keep these three Evangelical Counsels—poverty, chastity, and obedience—and he will indeed give up all his possessions, dressing himself in a base and poor garment; he will also submit to the obedience of some Superior, confining himself for these purposes in some **Cloister** or **Congregation** <note>Cloister/Congregation: Referring to a monastery or a religious community living under a specific rule</note> to regulate his life according to the commands of others; he will also leave behind the social life of the world in order to live in **continence** <term>continence: Refers here to sexual abstinence or celibacy</term>. For nature would have become so brutalized by its own **sensualities** <term>sensualities: Here, the focus on physical pleasures and worldly impulses that distract the soul from its spiritual origins</term> that it would no longer have remembered God, nor the divinity of its own soul. And so it was with all their other laws: none of them were given to them except to lead them back to the **Dependence on God** <term>Dependence on God: The central theme of this work, referring to the soul’s total reliance on the Creator for every breath and action, reversing the impulse toward self-will</term>, by making them recognize the various means by which they had withdrawn from Him. 88. Since this was neglected by them, He commanded them to make sacrifices of their animals and other things, so that they might always acknowledge that everything came from God. These visible and sensory things serve to remind him that, internally and spiritually, he must recognize that everything he has comes from God and belongs entirely to Him; so much so that he must never dispose of anything except according to His will and desires.

It is better to learn the truth while we have life, than to find ourselves deceived at death; and it is better to listen to the truth that reproves, than to flatter oneself to one's own ruin.

->PREFACE.<-

the same subject: so much so that it would be almost as well not to begin this reading at all, as not to finish it; furthermore, one might form errors regarding certain words or terms that are not according to the custom of Scholars <term>Scholars: The original French 'Ecoliers' refers to those trained in the formal, often rigid academic traditions of the universities (the 'Schools') of the time.</term>, who apply themselves more to eloquence than to the meaning of things; and through their impatience, they might wish to cast doubt on some very certain truths, and lose the fruit of the true light that can convert you to God.

One must also not follow their opinions <term>opinions: The French 'sentimens' here denotes intellectual judgments or personal views rather than emotions.</term> in a matter so important; for the opinions of all the men in the world differ in some way. This cannot come from the truth, which is always firm; since the opinions of men change at every moment, and if one wished to listen to and follow them, one rejects and despises the truth, which is God; and one wrongs one's soul, which would receive the truth by ceasing to listen to men, who are sometimes so preoccupied with their own opinions that they refuse to receive anything else, through a habitual stubbornness <term>stubbornness: 'opiniâtreté' suggests a dogmatic refusal to change one's mind despite evidence of divine truth.</term>; valuing what they have learned in the Schools <note>The 'Schools' refers to the established religious and philosophical institutions of the 17th century, which the author views as spiritually blind.</note> more than what God reveals. # ->PREFACE.<-

...condemn everyone. This is very far from the truth; for if it were in my power to save each and every person, I would willingly die as many times as there are souls in darkness, in order to give as many lives to rescue them as there are people I see perishing, to my great regret. One must never believe that my words could damn anyone: because damnation <margin>(q)</margin> <note>original: "(q) Isa. 66. v. 4." Reference to Isaiah 66:4: "I also will choose their delusions, and will bring their fears upon them; because when I called, none did answer..."</note> comes from the free will of each individual. Neither man nor devil can damn us—not even God himself—for He can do no evil, and damnation is the greatest evil of all evils. And thus, it must necessarily follow that it comes from our own will alone, and from no other.

If I had any desire to damn anyone, and if it were in my power, I would not strive to reveal the truth to everyone so that they might be saved by it; nor would I expose myself to the contempt and sufferings inflicted upon me by the enemies of truth. These enemies are so strong and powerful that they have nearly overwhelmed me with persecutions already. Indeed, if God did not wish for me to bring the truth to light for the enlightenment and salvation of many, I would have already died fifty times over from the poisons and sorcery <term>sorcery: The original French 'maléfices' refers to harmful magic or supernatural "mischief" intended to cause illness or death.</term> that those in league with the Devil <term>those in league with the Devil: 'Pactionaires du Diable' refers to people believed to have made a formal pact with Satan to do his bidding on earth.</term> had prepared for me. Thus, I have no reason to provoke so many enemies against myself if I truly desired the damnation of men. No, my dear Brother, I have never desired anything so much as your salvation, and even that of my enemies. I write only for that, and I pray only for that, knowing well that everything else must soon perish forever.

<summary>The author defends her motives, arguing that her harsh truths are intended for the salvation of souls rather than their condemnation. She asserts that damnation is a result of personal free will and reveals that she has survived numerous assassination attempts and dark magic while pursuing her mission.</summary>

that a Supreme Deity <term>Supreme Deity</term> <note>Bourignon frequently uses this term to refer to God as the ultimate, sovereign source of all existence.</note> could not have created the heavens & the earth, with everything inside them; nor formed our body, nor inspired our soul. As for knowing how that could be done, it matters little to us, provided that we render to God submission and dependence for all that he has given us, as something coming from him, and belonging to him. Then we shall have **living faith**, which can never be in a soul without producing love, respect, and submission to the Lord who gave us everything through his Omnipotence.

So it is that he who believes he has faith while he still holds an affection for his own <term>self-interest: the original French *propre interêt* refers to the ego's desire for personal gain, comfort, or status, which 17th-century spiritual writers saw as the primary obstacle to divine love.</term> is mistaken; for the light of faith makes it clear enough that we ought to possess nothing of our own; that everything belongs to God, and that everything we might have and possess in this world is transitory, perishable, and of short duration. Seeing by this light of faith that our soul is eternal and spiritual, it will not amuse itself by amassing gold and silver for the little time it must remain on the earth; and it will not weigh itself down with metals <note>In this context, "metals" refers to gold and silver coinage.</note> for its journey: for a **Pilgrim** travels more easily when he is unburdened than when he carries some heavy load.

**124.** He who has faith sees clearly that the goods of this world are a charge and very heavy to one who walks toward eternity. One must not be deceived into believing they have faith while they employ themselves, toil, and study to seek their own interest: because such a thing is directly repugnant to faith, which shows <gloss>(t)</gloss> the vanity of all these things, and produces a contempt for everything that is not spiritual and eternal. For faith, which is divine, cannot produce *human works* which look only to the earth, and can never move men to seek their own interests, nor to desire the goods of this world. By these testimonies, every person can well judge whether they have faith or not by their own conscience; and admit, if my saying is true, that there is no more faith in the world.

F 2

125. But as human nature always seeks to excuse itself, it will perhaps find some exception in the pursuit of its own <term>interest: specifically "self-interest" (*propre interet*), the 17th-century spiritual term for the ego's drive for personal security and gain at the expense of trust in God.</term>, under the pretext that these temporal goods are necessary for the maintenance of life. This certainly denotes that faith is dead in us, even though we are entirely persuaded that we have faith and that we are living well.

# PREFACE.

[...who] banishes us from the Paradise of delights to come and earn the sustenance of this miserable life with sweat; which we nevertheless wish to spend in delights and pleasures as much as possible. To believe that this is a place of <term>penance: the author refers to the "theological state of penitence," viewing earthly life as a period of spiritual labor and discipline following the Fall of Man.</term>, and yet to wish to dominate within it, is to act against one’s own belief, and even more against faith, which shows us the truth of all things so clearly that it would be impossible to live in the way people do now, if faith had remained in us. For one would effectively recognize that gold, silver, and earthly possessions are nothing but burdens, from which one would wish to be delivered; and that all the honors of the world are but a puff of wind, which dissipates instantly. One would hold these things in contempt; indeed, one would even feel displeasure at being honored, knowing that all honor is due to God alone. One would even avoid the pleasures of this life, knowing that this is only a time of penance, during which one would rejoice in sufferings so as to satisfy God—just as an honest debtor finds satisfaction in paying the debts he knows he truly owes.

<header>### Dead faith of today.</header>

<sig>F 3</sig>
<meta>catchword: dear</meta>

[...the fa]vor of being able to live so long that the light has come into the world, and being able to be enlightened by this new day, which dawns on our horizon. Never choose to love your darkness more than the light, even though it may be a bit hard for you to digest at the start. Doctors performing <term>effective treatments: the original "bonnes operations" refers to medical procedures or the working of a remedy.</term> usually cause pain and <term>griping: "tranchées," a term for sharp, colicky internal pains often associated in this period with the "cleansing" effects of strong medicine.</term>; but once they have achieved their effects, the body remains healthier and more cheerful. These truths will do the same for your soul if you are willing to incorporate them. I do not doubt that it will be a bit bitter for you to believe that you do not have *faith* when you are so firmly persuaded that you do; but one must surrender to the truth and leave the darkness to enter the light, rather than going from temporal darkness to eternal darkness. It is likewise harsh to speak of damnation to those who desire to be saved; but I would gladly sharpen your fine wit to answer me this question: namely, whether one can truly be saved without faith; and if faith can be living without <term>works: "œuvres," referring to the theological concept that true internal belief must be manifested through external actions and a specific way of life.</term>; and if these works should not have the qualities I have just detailed for you? And since the men of today do not have these works, they do not have faith; and not having faith, they cannot, as a consequence, have salvation; and to those to whom salvation does not belong, hell is their necessary lot: for there are only these two eternal lives, one blessed and the other miserable.

128. Must you not admit to me, my <note>The text cuts off here, continuing on the next page with the word "dear."</note>

<header>->**FOREWORD.**<-</header>

Dear Brother, are these things not true? And having acknowledged them, could you really still remain in doubt, or neglect to follow them, while desiring to be saved? This would be to act as an enemy to your own well-being, and to prefer a stubborn prejudice of the mind over your eternal happiness. If you persist in the belief that you and those like you are Christians and possess faith, you cannot believe that the world is judged: because God does not judge the universal world except <gloss>(y)</gloss> when faith is universally dead. It is so at present; and the Devil cannot have perfect dominion over all men except at the end of the <term>latter days: "derniers temps," referring to the eschatological belief that the world is in its final period before the Last Judgment.</term>, in which we are currently living without fearing it. I do not mean to say that all men in general are bound to the Devil by a specific pact; for there are still a few who are not. Nevertheless, he has such an influence over their souls as to blind them, so that they do not see that they are no longer true Christians, and that their faith is dead. It is to those people that I speak, so that they might emerge from their darkness and become children of light, by being reborn and returning to the womb of their Mother—which is the true original Faith that God infused into their souls when creating them.

\* 129. All those who wish to be saved must return to this faith, regardless of what sect, nation, or condition they may be in: for God is <term>no respecter of persons: "excepteur des personnes," a traditional theological phrase meaning God does not show favoritism based on social rank or nationality.</term>. Every kind of nation has some logic in what they believe; but none have the true faith. For whoever believes in God loves Him,
