# * The Necessity of Reclaiming Faith.

<sig>F 4</sig>
<meta>catchword: of</meta>

...loves Him, and follows Him. This is what no one does at present. The pagans have some reason for making idols to worship them; because, being far removed from faith, they cannot worship a God who is invisible to them. They make idols out of their most precious possessions so that they may see and touch what they adore, being unable to see or touch the God they nevertheless believe to be so powerful. This is why, in the past, God willed a Temple so rich and magnificent to be built in His honor; because they could not understand His Majesty and Grandeur except through material means. 

Atheists also have some reason to believe that there is no God when they have fallen so far from faith that they no longer know anything other than their <term>base senses: "sens brutaux," referring to physical instincts or perceptions unguided by spiritual reason.</term>, which can no more understand divine things than can the <term>beasts: "bestes," a common term for animals, implying a lack of a rational or immortal soul.</term>, since they have no light other than that of animal instinct. The Jews have some reason to continue the works of the Law when they do not have the light of Faith to see that they cannot be saved without it. 

The Calvinists, Lutherans, and all the other sects have some reason to follow their diverse opinions when they believe them to be good and more perfect, not discovering what the true Faith is. Those of the Roman Church also have some reason to believe they are saved by means of the Sacraments when they are not shown what the true Faith is. And thus, everyone perishes because of various human justifications, which will not be sanctioned by God; seeing that He gave to all men, in creating them, the light...

<summary>The author argues that all contemporary religious groups—including Pagans, Atheists, Jews, Protestants, and Catholics—are currently lost because they rely on material things, rituals, or human logic rather than the "true original Faith." Pagans are mistaken in worshiping metals as Gods. Atheists are mistaken in not recognizing God, despite seeing so many things that could not have been made by human hands. * *Useless Reformations. Necessary Renewal.*
F 5
<meta>catchword: tice</meta>

\* 131. For all the **Reformations** that God has made in the World are now turned to contempt; and instead of men having placed themselves back into **dependence upon God** through the sacrifices of beasts—which <gloss>foreshadowed</gloss> <margin>Romans 12, verse 1</margin> the <term>interior sacrifice: the mystical practice of offering one's own will and soul to God rather than performing physical rituals</term> that men were to make to God of themselves—they stopped at the <term>exterior sacrifices: physical rituals, such as animal offerings, which the author argues are meaningless without inner devotion</term>. They believed they had satisfied their debt by offering their beasts or other things, while reserving for themselves their own wills and desires, in order to dispose of them at their own wish. 

And when God wished to reform the world through the <term>Laws and exterior ceremonies: the religious rules and rituals established in the Old Testament</term> that he ordained for men through **Moses** and other **Prophets**, to distract them from the cares of the earth—which made them forget God and the honors and services they owed Him—they attached themselves to these exterior Ceremonies and Laws without applying their understanding to the **interior worship** they owed to God, of which the exteriors were merely the <note>original: "figure"</note> symbolic representation.

They likewise despised these things; and although they have heard and received them, they have nonetheless not followed them. *Obstacles to the **Faith of Dependence**.*

...to yourselves: *(b) Repent*, <note>original: "Faites penitence." While often translated as "do penance" in older Catholic versions, the author uses it here in the sense of a spiritual turning back to God.</note> *(c) Sell all that you have, and give it to the poor: and, (d) Be gentle and lowly and humble of heart.* It is not that he has any need for their wills: for he is more in himself than all the wills of a hundred thousand worlds. Nor has he any need of their penance; since he had created them to enjoy all things with pleasure, and he himself wishes to take his delights with them: nor likewise of their riches, since to him belongs everything, and he can give more to the poor than all men together. He has no more need of their humility, seeing that he had created them so noble and estimable: But he ordains all these things as remedies for their ills, applying various ointments to heal the various wounds he found in their souls, in order to restore them to the original health of the *Dependence on God*. If he had only said that they ought to return to this *Dependence*, they would not have been able to notice the specific things that had withdrawn them from it, such as their **self-love**, affection for riches, honors, and pleasures; and they could easily have been ignorant that all these things did not withdraw them from the Dependence on God, since today one is still very much in this ignorance after having seen and heard Jesus Christ do and teach the exact opposite: However, one still believes themselves to be abandoned to God while loving oneself, one's honors, riches, and pleasures.

* Explanation of the Scriptures.

is a direct opposition to the wisdom <note>original: "sapience." This term refers to a deep, divine wisdom that transcends mere intellectual knowledge.</note> of the Holy Spirit, who pours Himself out only upon humble hearts.

For whoever wishes to understand the Holy Scriptures by human wisdom will make great mistakes; seeing that many things are said there in terms that are not literal <note>original: "significatifs." The author argues that human language is often inadequate to describe divine actions directly.</note> to the thing God wants us to understand: because He seems sometimes to stutter, and to speak according to our own dialect <note>original: "pattoy" (patois). This suggests that God "dumbs down" His infinite truth into simple human language so we can grasp it.</note>. For example: He says He has <margin>(f) Exodus 10, verse 1</margin> **hardened the heart of Pharaoh**: This cannot mean that God hardens the hearts of men by making them obstinate in evil; for God can never cooperate with any evil, being the source of all good. Rather, He speaks this way to let us understand that He allows the wicked man to act in his wickedness when that man does not wish to withdraw from it. On His part, He always uses goodness toward them, as much through good inspirations as through admonitions and other means proper to their conversion. But when their free wills are determined to persist in evil, He lets them act, and says rather that *He* has hardened their heart than saying that *they* have hardened their heart against Him; this is because there is nothing that can ever harden itself or oppose itself against the Almighty.

137. This is why one must never stop at the words, but rather at their meaning; for if one wants to say that *God abandons us*, <margin>(g) Psalm 139, verse 8 <note>original: "Ps. 138." ->Dead Faith of the Present Day.<-

[to condemn] anyone, seeing that He is all goodness, from whom <margin>(l) <note>original: "Ps. 5. v. 5. Osée 13. v. 9."</note> Psalm 5:5, Hosea 13:9.</margin> no evils can come, however small they may be—far less would reprobation <note>In theology, reprobation is the state of being rejected by God and excluded from eternal salvation; it is the opposite of "election."</note> come from Him, which is the greatest of all evils. However, it is He who damns and reproves men indirectly when He exercises justice in their regard. His justice condemns all injustice, but it also rewards all justice: so that nothing is truer than that it is man who directly brings about his own punishment, who damns and reproves himself. And one can also say it is God indirectly, because it is He who always exercises justice. Should He cease to do so, no one would be damned; and conversely, if men did not commit injustice, God could not damn them; rather, He would have to reward them with eternal salvation; otherwise He would be neither just nor good. By this argument, one can understand that the terms of the Holy Scriptures are not to be understood in the way that people of today imagine; and when they say, *God has reproved Esau and chosen Jacob*; this signifies nothing other than that His justice gave sentence against the injustices of Esau, and that the same justice blessed the righteous acts of Jacob. For otherwise God would be neither good nor just, as I shall show in what follows.

139. All the difficulties that exist in understanding the Holy Scriptures arise from the fact that we do not know the attributes of God, and that we wish to attribute to Him those that are similar [to our own...]

### PREFACE.

...similar to men, thinking that He shows favor toward some and enmity toward others. This is not the case at all; but the ambition <note>In this 17th-century context, "ambition" refers to human pride and the ego's desire to be right or superior, rather than modern career goals.</note> of people today has deserved to be abandoned to the spirit of error, since no one is seen in the light of the truth. Everyone is wrapped up in their own personal interpretation and attached to their own opinions, upholding them as truths for which they would sometimes even give their lives—though in doing so, they would only be martyrs for the Devil. No one can be a martyr for Jesus Christ except the one who gives their life for the truth that is in God, not for that which is forged in the minds of men. The best course in this matter is always to take from the Holy Scriptures the meaning that draws us most toward the love and respect of God and the awareness of our own nothingness, and to leave aside all those things that seem debatable within the Wisdom of God <term>Wisdom of God (Sapience de Dieu): A term referring to the vast, inscrutable intelligence of the Divine that exceeds human logic.</term>, deeming ourselves unworthy of being able to understand what He is, how He performs His works, and by what means He saves men. We should hold fast to the faith which shows us that He does everything kindly, justly, and truly, without occupying ourselves with anything else but putting into practice the things He has openly declared to us through His Gospel. These are necessary for our salvation: namely, humility of heart, self-denial <note>original: "abnegation de soy mesme"</note>, the love of God, and charity toward one’s neighbor.

\* 141. For if one examines closely what both sides <note>Likely referring to the various theological factions of the 17th century (such as Molinists and Jansenists) who debated the nature of free will and grace.</note> propose to believe, it will be found that they all provide us with means to presume too much of ourselves, or else to relax our diligence in performing salutary works <term>Salutary works (œuvres salutaires): Deeds or actions performed through faith and grace that are considered beneficial for the soul's health and salvation.</term>. I speak here regarding the matter of Grace: for he who says *that man can have God’s grace to convert whenever he pleases* gives free rein to sinners, who then persist in their sins believing they can convert at the end of their lives, or at the hour of death. This belief that they can recover grace whenever they wish usually deceives them: for a will that has loved sin every day of its life cannot truly hate it at the moment of death. Thus, this doctrine is very dangerous, and far more harmful than beneficial; seeing as the belief that God’s grace is within our own power can never produce virtues in our souls, nor incite us to abandon ourselves to God or to do penance. It serves only to withdraw us from our dependence upon God

# F O R E W O R D.

...from God to trust in ourselves; and to delay our **conversion** <term>Conversion (conversion): In this context, the profound spiritual turning of the heart toward God and away from a life of sin.</term> until the very end of life, believing that it will then still be within our power to turn back to God.

142. This is greatly harmful; as is also the sentiment of those who say *that grace is given by God to one person and is denied to another: or, that one is chosen by God for salvation, and the other for rejection.* <note>The author is critiquing the doctrine of "double predestination"—the idea that God decides before birth who is saved and who is damned.</note> This directly shocks the **Goodness** and **Justice** of God, (*m*) which always walk uprightly, without having (*n*) **partiality** toward anyone; it also weakens the resolve of men to perform works that lead to salvation. For he who thinks he is **predestined** <term>Predestined (predestiné): The theological belief that a person's ultimate fate, whether heaven or hell, has been decreed by God from eternity.</term> to salvation will take no care for it, and will easily give himself over to every kind of license; for his salvation cannot fail him, no matter what he does. In the same way, he who believes he is rejected <note>original: "reprouvé" (reprobate). One who is excluded from God's grace and destined for damnation.</note> will not willingly do good, believing his rejection is certain, no matter what he does. Thus, it is quite evident that all these disputes and doctrines of men can bring nothing but great evils.

143. However, one sees the **learned**, who are even well-intentioned, apply themselves to these studies and disputes with fervor; thinking they are doing something pleasing to God and profitable for souls. But it is quite the opposite: for these things rob God of His honor, insofar as one attributes to men what belongs to God alone; and one gives room for despair, by making God out to be subject to the passions of...

---

(*m*) <note>original: "Deut. 32. v. 4."</note> Deuteronomy 32, verse 4: "He is the Rock, his works are perfect, and all his ways are just." 
(*n*) <note>original: "Rom. 2. v. 11."</note> Romans 2, verse 11: "For God does not show favoritism."

<summary>This page argues against the doctrine of predestination, suggesting that believing one's fate is fixed leads to either moral laziness or despair. The author emphasizes that God's justice is impartial and that theological disputes over grace often distract from actual spiritual practice.</summary>

# Opinions & doctrines of dangerous men.
<header>Opinions & doctrines of dangerous men.</header>
<sig>G 2</sig>

...of men, who love one and hate another according to their vicious impulses. This is what all these doctrines and disputes of men produce. They are certainly the main cause of the universal iniquity that we see at present in the world, where many would never dare commit so many kinds of sins if they were not supported and authorized by the doctrine of these men. Nature itself recoils at many things that these <term>Scholastic Doctors</term> <note>Scholastic Doctors: Medieval and early modern theologians who used formal logic and philosophy to analyze Christian doctrine, often criticized by reformers for being overly abstract and detached from simple faith.</note> declare to be permitted and lawful. Things have come so far that there are almost no abominations, however great they may be, for which some <term>Casuists</term> <note>Casuists: Theologians who resolve moral problems by applying general principles to specific "cases of conscience." The author implies they use "glosses" (interpretive notes) to find loopholes for sinful behavior.</note> do not find glosses to cover and excuse them!

144. In this regard, one must indeed call our time *dangerous and fearsome* with respect to our salvation. For by following the doctrine of those who are established in high offices, and seated in the <term>chair of Moses</term> <note>original: "chaire de Moïſe." A biblical reference to Matthew 23:2, referring to religious authorities who have the power to teach the law but may not practice what they preach.</note>, one is imperceptibly led to hell. This can be clearly discovered by examining to the very bottom what all these human studies and doctrines lead to. One will find that most study to raise themselves to positions of dignity and office; or to make themselves accomplished in the judgment of men; or else for profit or temporal advantages. All of which are things disagreeable to God, and detrimental to the salvation of those who apply themselves to these studies, as well as all the others who hear and follow these doctrines, since they result only in relaxing our practice of the virtues, instead of inciting us toward them:

<summary>The author argues that contemporary theological disputes and the "casuistry" of scholastic doctors provide excuses for sin, leading believers toward spiritual ruin rather than virtue. # FOREWORD.

...because men who claim to satisfy or please other men will always strive to seek reasons and arguments to support their feelings or <term>natural inclinations</term> <note>natural inclinations: The author refers to the "fallen" state of humanity, where our basic instincts are seen as being in opposition to divine will.</note>; and in doing so, they draw them away from God, because everything that is natural *(n)* is repugnant to His grace. However, today we see so many cases proposed and resolutions given regarding the very things that nature loves and seeks.

145. For the sake of brevity, I cannot report all these cases in particular here, referring everyone instead to the observation of the lives of men today: one will be proud all his life; another greedy; another, a drunkard or a glutton; another lustful; and so on for the rest, as anyone can plainly see. Yet all these people in general, and each in particular, will say that they want to go to Paradise and be saved. This hope and desire for salvation can come from nowhere else but the persuasion of these <term>Casuists</term>, who know so well how to accommodate every kind of sin that they no longer consider them sins at all. Instead, they call them virtues, or at the very least they call them <term>venial sins</term> <note>venial sins: In traditional theology, these are "forgivable" or minor sins that do not lead to the death of the soul (damnation), as opposed to "mortal" sins. The author is criticizing teachers who use this category to downplay serious moral failings.</note>, which they say are not enough to cause damnation. Thus, they lead many souls to their ruin—souls who, having never heard any of these "cases" or definitions, would have been in fear for their salvation and could find no rest in their sins if these <term>Doctors</term> <note>Doctors: Refers to "Doctors of the Church" or high-ranking academic theologians whose interpretations carried legal and spiritual authority.</note> did not gloss over and authorize them.

146. It is not without reason that the <term>Holy Spirit</term> has said:

<margin>*(n)* Romans 8: verses 6, 7. <note>original: "Rom. 8. v. 6, 7." These verses state that "the mind governed by the flesh is death, but the mind governed by the Spirit is life and peace."</note></margin>

## Rule for interpreting Scripture. Example.

...says: <margin>(o) Jeremiah 17:5.</margin> (o) *Woe to the man who puts his trust in man*, seeing as all are blind in what concerns divine things. Nothing can be known except through the <term>Light of Faith</term> <note>Light of Faith: In mystical and contemplative theology, this refers to a supernatural insight granted by God that transcends human logic or physical perception.</note>; and as long as this light is living, it discovers and follows divine things. But it is never known through the <term>natural senses</term>, which extend only to the limits of nature. For this reason, many have made great mistakes and fallen into error by wanting to understand divine things through their human judgments, or to interpret the Holy Scriptures, to which they give as many different meanings as their various opinions or inclinations lead them. They will also find many difficulties and contradictions there, because nothing can be truly understood except through the Light of Faith.

147. Thus, those terms which seem to say that God <term>reproves</term> <note>reproves: In this context, it refers to the theological concept of reprobation, where God is seen as rejecting or predestining certain souls to damnation.</note>, that he damns, and that he causes men to perish, cannot be understood by these literal words. Rather, they can only be understood by their [true] significance, which can only be known through the Light of Faith, and not otherwise. If we were to take these terms according to our own [human] sense, we would be speaking insults to God, and we would believe that he is furious or evil. This cannot be, because he is all goodness, and all peace and tranquility. Therefore, anyone who does not wish to do an injustice to God must never uphold the terms of the Scriptures except insofar as they lead us to love and adore God, or to the knowledge of our own <term>Nothingness</term> <note>Nothingness: Often rendered as "le Néant" in French spirituality, this refers to the humble recognition that the creature is nothing in comparison to the Creator and has no power of its own.</note>, and the <term>charity</term> of our neighbor. For God has said or taught us nothing

<summary>This page establishes that the Bible must be interpreted through the "Light of Faith" rather than human logic, which is blind to divine things. The author argues that passages describing a wrathful or punishing God must be understood spiritually to preserve the truth of God’s absolute goodness.</summary>
<keywords>Scripture, interpretation, Light of Faith, human judgment, divine nature, Nothingness, charity, Jeremiah 17:5</keywords>

# ->FOREWORD<-

(p) ...except for the things that lead to these virtues. Everything else is vanity. But when we believe that all grace and salvation come immediately from God (which is true) because we are <term>Nothings</term> <note>original: "Neants." In 17th-century mystical theology, this refers to the "creaturely nothingness" of human beings—the idea that humans have no independent power or goodness without God's constant support.</note>; then we honor God, and recognize His Omnipotence in making such great things from nothing through His grace. It is necessary that we also love Him for this reason, and pray to Him so that He continues and increases this grace within us. And when we believe that God is so kind toward us that as soon as (q) the sinner repents by returning to Him, He receives them in mercy; this serves to strengthen our love for Him, in consideration of the love He bears for us by pardoning us so mercifully. In these two senses, one can hold different opinions on grace: both believing that it comes entirely from God, and also believing that man can have it whenever he wishes, since God never rejects the repentant sinner. But to hold one or the other view in terms that do not aim to humble us and recognize the greatness of God—to love and adore Him—is a bad thing. It is a great boldness for man to interpret and corrupt the Scriptures with terms that authorize a <term>loosening of virtue</term> <note>This refers to "moral laxity" or finding excuses in theology to live a less-than-holy life.</note>.

148. For if one wishes to <term>gloss</term> <note>gloss: To provide a superficial or overly literal interpretation of a text's words while missing the underlying spiritual message.</note> upon the words of Scripture, one would say that God lied when He said to Jonah (r) that *within forty days the City of Nineveh would be overturned*; because the thing did not—

### ->Rule for interpreting Scripture. Example.<-

One must not, however, entrap God in His words, nor in the terms of the **Scriptures**: but one must draw from them the fruits for our souls in accordance with God’s design, who speaks to us only for our utility. He said absolutely that **Nineveh** would be submerged: because the inhabitants of that city had deserved this condemnation. The **Justice of God** had condemned their injustice; but as soon as they repented, and embraced **penance**, His goodness and **mercy** pardoned them once more. One must not say for this reason that God lied, but rather that He had judged them justly, and that He has since pardoned them mercifully; without wishing to <term>quibble</term> <note>original: "pointiller." To argue over trivial points or literal wording while missing the larger truth.</note> over the form of His words or terms, which contribute nothing to the meaning of things, except to make us understand them crudely according to our capacity and weakness.

In this, one finds no exception for ignorance, but rather a more blamable malice, for having wished to believe and speak of divine things as if we possessed them while we are very far removed from them.

# FOREWORD

<header>* To Know Without Doing.</header>
<sig>G 5</sig>

...vain in our time, where such a great number of people study mystical and divine things; and yet so few are divinized <note>The term "divinized" refers to the process of *theosis*, where a person is transformed by divine grace to become more like God in their nature and actions.</note>. They are like bells, calling others to go to Church, while they themselves always remain outside. Do not be among this number, my dear Brother: for it would profit you nothing to hear and understand all the wonders of God that I wish to declare to you, if this knowledge does not generate within your soul <gloss>(u)</gloss> the Love of God and the knowledge of yourself: for the wicked and those allied with the Devil now speak of the highest divine mysteries to more easily deceive the good, whom they could not win over by any evil things: but under the pretext of holiness, he <note>Referring to the Devil or the spirit of deception.</note> stops the best-intentioned, keeping them fixed in the speculation <note>In this context, "speculation" refers to purely intellectual study or theory that remains in the mind without affecting the heart or behavior.</note> of divine things without ever putting them into practice, persuading them that it is enough to have faith and virtue through speculation alone. This is why so few essentially possess virtue and a living faith.

152. However, the works they have performed since then have clearly testified that the Devil had been their master. Therefore, be...

# FOREWORD

...be on your guard, and seek nothing out of curiosity, but only to profit from it. I will not tell you speculative discourses, or things drawn from dreams, visions, or imaginations; but rather those which are fathered by a living faith <note>original: "vive foy"</note>: for I have never learned anything anywhere else. This faith has been my Master and my only Director. You will be able to experience this well through the effect these discourses will have in your soul, provided they find no obstacles: because everything that comes from living faith is always active <note>The author uses "operant," suggesting that true faith is not passive but produces immediate spiritual effects.</note>.

\* 153. Never believe that I have in all my claims any other interest than your salvation <term>salvation: the spiritual deliverance from sin and its consequences</term>: for as for riches, I can desire none, since those I possess as my own are a great burden to me: and as for honors, I can likewise wish for none, if indeed I wish to follow **Jesus Christ**: I actually prefer contempt, to be more conformed to my Savior. Neither do I affect to be esteemed and followed; because the opinions of men are of no account to me <note>The author indicates that human opinions are "mépris" (contemptible or worthless) compared to divine truth.</note>; for I know them to be so poorly founded in truth, and their conversations and social interactions serve as an obstacle to me: being unable to find delights and pleasures outside of solitude and interior recollection <term>interior recollection: a state of prayerful stillness where the soul withdraws from external distractions to focus on the presence of God within</term>. I am also not sent to reprimand the world; but to illuminate those who wish to receive the truth. If I rebuke anything in the lives of Christians today, it is because of their great blindness: for if I did not point out their faults in particular, they would persuade themselves that they are well illuminated, and would be unable to profit from the true light.

# * Goal and Conduct of the Author.

<meta>catchword: to be</meta>

...light while remaining in their darkness.

\* 154. In this, I imitate the way God has always acted to bring men back to their duties, and to make them return to their **Dependence on God** <term>Dependence on God: A central concept for the author, referring to the soul's total submission and return to a state where it is governed by Divine Will rather than self-will</term>, from which they had strayed. When He wished to bring them back through **Moses**, He gave them Laws and reprimands, so that they might first know their sins; and secondly, that they might amend them <note>The original French "amendassent" implies both correcting a fault and improving one's character.</note> by keeping the Laws and rules He gives them as fitting means to maintain them under the **Dependence of God**. And when **Jesus Christ** was on earth, He rebuked the **Pharisees** <note>original: "Pharisiens"; here used as a symbol for those who are outwardly religious but inwardly corrupt.</note>, so that they would first recognize their faults, and afterwards He taught them the means by which they could amend them. In the same way, it seemed necessary to me to reprimand the faults of the **Christians** of today: not that I come to condemn them; but rather to save them through the knowledge of the truth: for their condemnation lies in the falsehood <note>original: "mensonge," implying a state of spiritual self-deception.</note> in which they walk without perceiving it. And if this is not shown to them precisely and particularly, they will not be able to see how far they are removed from the **Dependence of God**; otherwise, I would have spoken of their particular errors in vain.

\* 155. If it had been sufficient to say that one must return to the **Dependence on God**, **Jesus Christ** would have said nothing else in His **Gospel**: since all His teachings lead to no other end. He would not do anything superfluous by saying so many diverse things. It is certain that all these diverse things needed to be...

-># FOREWORD.<-

...to be taught by **Jesus Christ** for our salvation; likewise it seemed necessary to me to touch upon, in this **FOREWORD**, several faults and sins in which the **Christians** of today are mired. If these are not uncovered, it will be impossible for them to effectively enter into the light of the **Truth**. For even though they might see that God created them to take His delights with them, and that they must abandon themselves <term>Abandonment (abandonner): A spiritual practice of surrendering one's self-will and personal desires entirely to God's providence.</term> entirely to Him, they might nonetheless persuade themselves that they are doing all these things simply because they say them in words. Indeed, one hears nothing more commonly said than that they love God and wish to be entirely abandoned to Him, while in reality nothing is less likely. For everyone follows their own will and governs themselves by their natural instinct, and thinks, despite this, that all is well and that they will be saved.

It is therefore to undeceive them that I have succinctly declared several errors in which they live; not by any *reprehension* <note>original: "reprehension"; implying a harsh or formal rebuke intended to punish.</note>, but by pure *admonition* <note>original: "admonition"; a gentle warning or counsel intended to improve the listener.</note>. For I do not wish to dominate anyone, nor to master whomever it may be; seeing as God has given to each their **free will**, it is not fitting for me nor for anyone else to bridle or constrain it: each must dispose of it according to the choice they make. Men must not take captive what God has willed to leave free. This is why no one will have reason to bear me ill-will for rebuking these errors, since it is only by way of admonition, and not by correction.

156. Perhaps someone will ask me why I rebuke more the faults and errors of the...

->### The Aim and Conduct of the Author.<-

...[think I am targeting] **Christians**, rather than those of all other **Religions**, who seem even more reprehensible; seeing as I wish to speak to everyone in general: [or] that I should not offend **Christians**, or those of the **Roman Church** in particular? To which I respond that two things move me to this: The first, that the **Roman Church** is my Mother, in whom I was begotten; and for this I am obliged to love her more than any others; and the effect of this love is the reason that I reveal her faults and errors; so that she may purge herself of the faults that she might perhaps be unaware of if they were not brought to light, or specifically declared. The second cause and reason why I reveal the faults of **Christians** more than those of other **Religions** is because I know no other **Sects** or **Religions**; and because I have never associated with <note>original: "pratiqué"; here meaning to have social or spiritual dealings with people.</note> other persons than those who made profession of the **Roman Catholic Apostolic Faith**: having been born and lived more than fifty years under her obedience alone, without ever having known another **Church**, whether by word or by writing: this is why I cannot rebuke the particular failings of those who are not under the same profession, having neither known nor associated with them.

## FOREWORD.

[...to be] taught by Jesus Christ for our salvation, likewise it seemed necessary to me to touch upon in this **FOREWORD** several faults and sins in which the Christians of today are mired; <note>original: "assis," literally "seated."</note> for if these are not uncovered, it will be impossible for them to enter effectively into the light of the **Truth**. For even though they might see that **God** created them to take His delight in them, and that they ought to abandon themselves entirely to Him, they might nevertheless persuade themselves that they are doing all these things because they do them in words alone. Indeed, one hears nothing more common than people saying they love **God** and wish to be entirely surrendered to Him, while nothing could be further from the truth. For everyone follows their own will and is governed by their natural instinct, yet thinks despite this that all is well and that they will be saved. It is therefore to undeceive them that I have briefly declared several errors in which they live—not by way of *reprehension*, but by pure *admonition*. For I do not wish to dominate anyone, nor to master any person whomsoever, seeing as **God** has given to everyone their free will. It is not fitting for me, nor for anyone else, to bridle or constrain it; each must dispose of it as they choose. Men must not hold captive what **God** has willed to leave free. This is why no one will have reason to bear me ill will for rebuking these errors, since it is done only by way of admonition, and not by correction.

156. I will perhaps be asked why I rebuke the faults and errors of Chris-

->### Goal and Conduct of the Author.<-

To which I answer that two things move me to this: The first, that the **Roman Church** is my Mother, in which I was begotten; <note>original: "engendrée." The author uses familial language to express her deep-rooted connection to the Catholic Church.</note> and for that reason I am obliged to love her more than any other; and the effect of this love is the reason that I uncover her faults and errors; so that she may purge herself of the faults that she might perhaps be unaware of if they were not brought to light, or declared in private. The second cause and reason why I uncover the faults of **Christians** more than those of other **Religions** is because I do not know any other **Sects** or **Religions**; and I have never associated with <note>original: "pratiqué," meaning to have social or spiritual dealings with.</note> any other persons than those who made profession of the <term>Catholic Apostolic Roman Faith</term>; <note>This is the formal title of the Roman Catholic Church, emphasizing its claim to historical continuity from the Apostles.</note> for having been born and having lived for more than fifty years under its sole obedience, without ever having known another **Church**, neither by word nor by writing: this is why I cannot rebuke the particular defects of those who are not under the same profession, for I have neither known nor associated with them.

161. One must not think, however, that I am a heretic, or that I hold Roman Catholics in low esteem. And if I held any heresies, I would have to be their first inventor, as I have never learned what heresy might be. I have always remained Catholic, Apostolic, and Roman in the Spirit of Jesus Christ, just as the first Christians were.

->### Uses of this Book.<-

If heresies or errors have since crept into this Roman Church, or elsewhere among Christians, I detest and abhor them, while standing firm upon the **living faith** <note>original: *foy vive*. A central concept in 17th-century mysticism, referring to an active, internal relationship with God rather than mere adherence to outward rituals.</note>, which I wish for all people in the world, since without it no one can ever be saved. This is something everyone admits in words, yet no one believes in practice; for the effects of this faith would produce true and perfect Christians. Such people are found nowhere. Since the age of four, I have desired to find a country where true Christians lived, and I have not until now come to the knowledge of one, despite having sought information for so long.

162. I hope, however, to encounter it after this **GOD'S LAST MERCY** <note>The title of the author's work, which she presents as a final prophetic revelation to restore true Christianity before the end of the age.</note> is spread throughout the world. For I believe it will also work within the hearts of the *Pagans*, who will come through it to the knowledge of the true God who created them, and will break all their idols, which they will see are powerless. I do not doubt that it will also strike a chord in the souls of the *Jews*; because, seeing that God has never required anything from any person other than the **DEPENDENCE** of their wills on His own essentially, and that all the other faiths <note>original: *foys*. The author implies that specific religious laws or dispensations were temporary additions necessitated by human sin, rather than God's primary intent.</note> He has given them since then have only been accidental, because of their sins; once they have understood this well, they will have no trouble receiving the light of Truth which they formerly rejected and failed to recognize, in order to leave behind all the works of the law to embrace the **LIVING FAITH**, which brings about the **SURRENDER** <term>abandonment: (French: *abandon*) in this mystical context, it refers to "abandonment to divine providence," or the total yielding of the human will to the divine will.</term> of our will to that of God, which is the only...

\* 164. Perhaps it will be surprising that I make such a long speech in this FOREWORD. It is not, however, that I am wordy in my discourse; but rather only because I have many things to say before entering into the subject matter to discover Truth in its source and its origin. Because the people of today are so far removed from it, that if one did not first show them this *estrangement*, it would be impossible for them to know or understand the essence of these truths without having first discovered their errors; and also to admonish all those who wish to benefit from this reading, to surrender themselves to *God*, and to detach themselves from themselves. When He wishes me to share them, He gives me the terms and the words suited to expand upon them to others, so that they might understand them clearly. In this way, everything comes from the same source: the insight, the understanding of it, and the manner of expressing it to others. I always feel rejoiced when some passage from the **Holy Scripture** comes to my mind so that my words might be better understood or carry more authority; seeing that many believe in the Scripture who do not believe as much in my own words, which are only known to a few individuals to whom I have personally shared them.

<margin>168.</margin> This is why I willingly use the terms of Scripture when I can discover them, as it is a more universal doctrine than the writings of a single person; yet the latter is of the same substance as that which is in the **Holy Scriptures** when it is guided by the **Holy Spirit**. For this Holy Spirit is always uniform and consistent with Himself; and what He dictated in the **Holy Scriptures**, and what He dictates now to <term>purified souls</term> <note>original: "ames épurées"; souls that have been cleansed of worldly attachments and ego through spiritual discipline.</note>, is surely the same thing: because no <term>change</term> <note>original: "mutation"; the theological concept that God's nature and truth are immutable and do not vary over time.</note> can ever be found in God. Thus, if what I put forward is not in conformity with the **Holy Scripture**, it must not be believed.

But before engaging further in this **Eternal Covenant**, or in this **glorious Kingdom of Jesus Christ**, I would like, my dear Brother, to have you re-enter your mother’s womb <note>A reference to the Gospel of John 3:3-4, where Jesus tells Nicodemus that one must be "born again" to see the Kingdom of God.</note>, so that you might be reborn and become like a little child. For all men may err, however holy they may be; but the truth can have neither error nor change. It must, however, be drawn from its origin, and all things must be known by their beginnings. To this, one must submit one's mind like little children; otherwise, one will never come to the knowledge of this truth, especially when one is preoccupied with contrary sentiments caused by readings or <term>doctrines</term> <note>original: "Doctrines apparantes"; teachings that have the outward appearance of truth but lack its divine essence.</note> that only appear to be this truth. # FOREWORD.

...bastards of some creature; some have taken for their Father **Calvin**, **Luther**, or some other **Sectarians**; others have taken a **Dominic**, a **Francis**, an **Augustine**, and so many others of various orders among the **Romanists** <note>A term used by the author to refer to followers of the Roman Catholic Church.</note>. Believe me, my dear Brother, we are not the true children of all these Fathers; since we have only one common Father of all men, who is God, who alone created them, and can alone save them; but neither **Pope**, nor **Councils**, nor **Doctors** <term>Doctors</term> <note>original: "Docteurs"; referring to the "Doctors of the Church," highly influential theologians and scholars.</note>, with all the others, can do either one or the other of these things, for all men together would not know how to create a single body, nor save a single soul, whatever they say and promise us.

Therefore it is necessary, like the **prodigal son** <note>A reference to the biblical parable in Luke 15:11-32, symbolizing a sinner's return to God.</note>, to return to our true common Father, who is God, and to repent for having left our dependence on Him to subject ourselves to these illegitimate Fathers, who have led us like galley slaves under the slavery of their opinions, making us recognize as true whatever their own inclinations could stir up. They have even often caused us to consume all the graces and lights we had received from God, only to follow and support them through diligent study and insatiable pains and labors.

This is why it is more than time to reclaim the **Liberty of the true children of God**, and to consider where we came from, by whom we were created, and for what purpose, so that following this true consideration, we may recover the light of **true Faith**, which will show us the truth of all things.

### CHAPTER ONE.

*That there is a God: That men deny this truth by their life and their practice; and only confess it through speculations and through the words of a dead and sterile faith.*

I state as a first foundation that there is a God, who has created all things. Many will grant me this first proposition: for all Christians say in the first article of their creed, *to believe in God Almighty, Creator of Heaven and earth.* The Jews, Turks <note>In the 17th century, "Turks" was frequently used as a general term for all Muslims, particularly those within the Ottoman Empire.</note>, and so many other nations confess the same thing; indeed, even the Pagans and savage <note>A common historical term used by authors of this period to describe indigenous peoples or those living outside of Western "civilized" structures.</note> peoples are obliged to recognize that there is a God who governs all things: because by seeing the constitution of the World with everything that is within it, they can see by their rational judge-

<meta>catchword: Being</meta>

-ment that it is necessary for there to be a God who has given being and beginning to all these things; since neither man nor any other creatures would know how to create a blade of grass or a grain of wheat; even less a vein, a muscle, a nail, or a hair of the human body; nor would they know how to make the least of all these things subsist for a single moment. Thus, there must be a Supreme Power who creates, rules, and maintains the whole, and sustains them. For in considering the Sun, the Moon, the Stars, the Elements, the Earth, the Sea, the Beasts, the Fruits, and all that we see here below; each of these things in particular and all in general declare that there is a God, without whom nothing could have being or support. Indeed, man, merely by looking at himself, is obliged to confess that there is a God who created him and who maintains him: for no one made himself: everyone knows well that there was a time in which he did not exist; and when he was made, he knows not how: for all the industry of all the men who ever lived is not capable of the slightest craftsmanship or ingenuity <note>original: "ingenie" — refers to the cleverness or complex engineering found in nature.</note> that one sees in the <term>construction: the original term "fabrique" refers to the anatomical structure or "workmanship" of the body</term> of the human body, nor of making any work that could approach the perfection and the fulfillment of the proportions of that same body, no more than anyone would know how to make it subsist in life for a single moment.

For if we believed there is a God to whom all these things belong, and if we truly held these convictions, we would unfailingly return ourselves and everything we possess to Him as His own property, which He should dispose of at His good pleasure. Yet we do the complete opposite: for instead of being voluntarily surrendered to His will, we find it difficult to bear that His will be accomplished in us when it does not conform to our own inclinations or feelings. This is why I have rightly established as the first <term>foundation stone: the essential core principle upon which all other truths rest (pierre fondamentale)</term>, *that there is a God, Creator of all things*; in order to show everyone that each person is ignorant of this truth, which is so necessary for <term>salvation: the spiritual rescue from sin and its consequences (salut)</term>; and so that no one perishes through this ignorant belief, that Faith consists

### CHAPTER TWO.

#### *On the Attributes of God, who, being incomprehensible, reveals Himself through His works: His Power, His Goodness, His Justice, and His Truth.*

Secondly, I would very much like to show the qualities of God: not those He possesses in His <term>Essence: the fundamental nature or "inner being" of God (essence)</term>, which are incomprehensible; such that no one can ever see or understand God; because He is <term>Pure Spirit: a being without physical form or matter (pur esprit)</term>; and our understanding, being joined and united so closely to our material body, has no capacity to comprehend things purely divine and spiritual. To understand anything of them, it would be necessary for our soul, which is pure spirit, to be untied and separated from our body, or else for this body to be <term>Divinized: transformed or elevated to a divine state (divinisé)</term>. In that case, one might perhaps understand something of the divine qualities of God: but as long as we live in this miserable life, we can understand nothing except what is visible or sensible of the divine qualities.

2. This is why many have erred and "broken their heads" <note>original: "se sont rompu la teste" — an idiom meaning to strain one's intellect to the point of exhaustion or frustration</note> wanting to understand what God is. They fall into the same sin that Adam fell into shortly after his creation. It is not

<summary>This page introduces Chapter Two, arguing that God’s essential nature is incomprehensible to the human mind because of our physical limitations. The author suggests that we can only know God through His visible works and warns that attempting to intellectually master God's essence is a form of pride akin to the fall of Adam.</summary>
<keywords>divine attributes, incomprehensibility of God, pure spirit, material body, living faith, original sin, human understanding, essence of God</keywords>

-># On the Attributes of God.<-
I 3
<meta>catchword: domin-</meta>

to believe that the beauty or goodness of an apple tempted him to satisfy the sense of taste, seeing that during the <term>State of Innocence: the theological concept of humanity's condition before the Fall, characterized by perfect harmony between the soul and God (état d'innocence)</term> he could not be subject to any natural sensualities: for his spirit then held dominion over his flesh and his passions, just as he held it over all other creatures; but he committed this spiritual sin of ambitiously desiring to know good and evil. Now all good is in God; and all evil comes from the will of man: which he learned well by experience, feeling himself overwhelmed by all sorts of evils after having followed his own desire; but he was frustrated in his pretension of wanting to know the good: because the latter was incomprehensible to his understanding. In the same way will be, and have been, all men who have wanted and want to understand the <term>Essence: the fundamental nature or "whatness" of a being (essence)</term> of God, or the divine conditions that are in Him, or that concern Him: because no creatures, whatever they may be, celestial or earthly, Angelic or human, are capable of understanding the least thing of what God is, and what is in His essence.

# The Last Mercy of God.

...the domain of our will, our soul is then Paradise. If He ruled over the earth, the earth would then be Paradise; likewise if He ruled over the sea, even in <term>Hell: here referring to the underworld or the depths (Enfers)</term>. For the dominion of God creates Paradise in every place where He resides, because He is not restricted to any specific location, since all things are lesser than He; and nothing can be contained within something that is smaller than itself. A large horse cannot have the <note>original: "pertuy d'une petite éguille"</note> eye of a small needle for its stable, nor can a great house be built upon a single foot of ground. It must always be that what is contained is smaller than that which contains it. How then can anyone claim that Heaven is specifically the Paradise where God resides, since that very Heaven is but His <term>Creature: any created thing, as opposed to the Creator (Creature)</term>, which possesses no proportion that could equal or encompass its <term>Creator: the divine source of all existence (Createur)</term>? All these words and various terms found even in the <term>Holy Scriptures: the Bible (Saintes Ecritures)</term> are merely ways of speaking to allow our weakness to grasp roughly something of the qualities of the <term>Eternal Good: a name for God emphasizing His infinite goodness (Bien Eternel)</term>. And when it is said that Paradise is in Heaven, it is only to make it understood that <term>Union with God: the state of spiritual oneness with the Divine (Union avec Dieu)</term> is a good above all other goods, using the metaphor that Heaven is above the earth and all other coarse, material bodies. For to find words or terms capable of expressing Divine matters is impossible: since our words are material entities, they cannot through explicit terms convey spiritual things, because of the vast disproportion between them.

8. ### 9. The second Quality that we can know in God is His *Goodness*,

which is also discovered through His works: for everything He has made is full of goodness. It is true that we see much <term>malignity: evil, malice, or the tendency to cause harm</term> in the works of God; but it does not originate from Him, but rather from the sin that man has committed. For God can never do anything evil; and everything He has made, and still makes, is very good and perfect. It would be very easy for us to judge this if we could see all His works as they were created from the beginning. We would have to confess that everything was good and perfect, without any evils. ### 10.
Notwithstanding everything that man could have done, we still see today that in all of God’s works there is something of His **Goodness**, which floats above even the <term>malignity: the inherent evil or harmful nature</term> that man has brought to them. Consequently, one will find in all of God’s creatures more good than evil; because man’s malice can never overcome God’s goodness. To discover this goodness, one must first notice what goodness He showed in the **Creation of man**. He, who was the **Supreme Good**, independent of everything, could have no need of anything. Nevertheless, He occupied Himself with crafting a world in order to place there a creature with whom He wished to take His delights and pleasures. Now, neither necessity nor the desire for delight could have induced Him to create man, seeing that, containing all things within Himself, nothing can be necessary to Him; and being the perfect Good, He possessed all delights within Himself. Rather, it was His overabundant **Goodness** alone among His qualities that made Him want to create a Creature with whom He desired to share His delights; and this was done through pure goodness. For man, before having his <term>being: his existence or life</term>, could not move or stir Him toward his creation, seeing as he was nothing, and could do nothing to attract this goodness to give him existence.

For although by His **Omnipotence** <term>Omnipotence: God’s all-powerful nature</term> He could have sustained man through spiritual and supernatural means, He still wished—by an overflow of goodness—to give him visible, perceptible, and material creatures to refresh, serve, and sustain his body, which is likewise material. He did this so that everything He had placed within man might find its perfect delight in every fulfillment: the **Soul**, which is Divine and Spirit, finding joy in the **Divinity**; and the material bodies finding joy in sensory and material objects suited to their nature. 

Thus, the **Goodness of God** is revealed in how He gives to man—who is but a tiny <term>nothingness: a common 17th-century theological term (un petit néant) emphasizing the creature's total dependence on God</term>—all kinds of creatures to satisfy and bring pleasure even to the least of his senses. For the sun refreshes the eye by allowing it to see so many diverse objects; the voices and sounds of men, of birds, and so many other things that fill the air, bring contentment to hearing; fruits, foods <note>original: "viandes," which in this period refers to food or sustenance in general</note>, and drinks bring pleasure to taste; flowers and sweet scents do the same for smell; and so it is with all the rest.

That is just, good, and reasonable.

2. To this end, He gave him qualities worthy of this communication <note>In 17th-century theology, "communication" refers to God sharing His own nature or essence with humanity.</note>, creating in him a divine and eternal soul, in no way unlike Himself except in that it is His Creature; whereas He is an uncreated Deity, who existed from all eternity, producing this likeness of His at a certain point in time, so that from then on it might remain eternally like God. And because there could be no true resemblance between an infinite God and a finite creature, He created in this soul three divine and incomprehensible powers, just as they are in God. And although we call them memory, understanding, and will <note>These three "faculties of the soul" were famously defined by St. Augustine as a reflection of the Holy Trinity within the human person.</note>, no one can truly understand what they are, any more than we understand what they do in God, except for the effects these powers produce—both those of God and those of the human soul.

3. The effect of God's memory produces the recollection of everything that has been from all eternity; and the effect of man’s memory is to remember what it has known since its creation. The effect of God’s understanding results in His hearing and comprehending all things; and the effect of man’s understanding is to comprehend that there is an All-just, All-good, and All-powerful God, who created all things. The effect of God’s will is the Love and benevolence He bears toward man, having willed that he be made like Him; and the effect of man’s will is that it inclines toward loving his God, his Creator.

4. There, my dear Brother, is the sole reason why God created man: so that He might have a creature capable of receiving His goodness and sharing His delights, wishing to love and be loved by it. Not that He had any need of anything to find His delights, since He has possessed them from all eternity within Himself; but because He wished to share His delights with others besides Himself, and through a love of benevolence, allow something outside of Himself to participate in His goodness.

5. Man was thus created by Him for this sole end, and was composed of a body and a soul, both immortal and eternal, so as to remain always in the company of God, to enjoy together eternal delights that shall never end. ### The Last Mercy of God.

[have] been created both immortal to live eternally: for no works of God will ever perish. Since He is an eternal God, everything He makes is, consequently, all eternal works. So it is that all the creatures He has made shall remain for all eternity: each in their **species** <note>original: "especes." In this context, it refers to the "kinds" or "types" of beings as established at the moment of creation.</note>, just as God created them: nothing being able to perish of the works of His hands. All shall be as eternal as they are good: nothing evil being able to come from the source of all goodness; no more can anything perish from the source of Eternity.

6. I fear, my Brother, that your mind may have a little difficulty in conceiving this proposition: because you are preoccupied with other ideas that men have persuaded you of until now, and also by the experience of seeing men and beasts die daily, which are nevertheless creatures of God. But give yourself the patience to understand my words well: you will find that there is nothing truer than the proposition I put before you, provided you take truths from their source, and things from their origins. For the reason why men say the world will end with everything inside it, is because they do not know God, nor His works: and this ignorance makes them speak of it just as blind men do of colors, which they have never seen. # Of the Qualities of God.
->K 2<-

men of today, having fallen into blindness through <term>sin</term> <note>original: "peché." 7. If it is held to be a great injustice to reduce to nothing the works of a man whom one considers learned or holy; how much more unjust would it be to destroy or annihilate the works of a God?

8. Men, through their impieties, would surely destroy—as much as it would be in their power—the works of God through their malice; but to believe that God Himself, who is as good as He is just, would not have made His works to be eternal, would be to derogate from His <term>Justice</term>, and even from <term>reason</term>: for every species of creature always produces its own kind; thus the works of God all have some resemblance to Him. 14. If God did this at the beginning of the world for the body of man—which was capable of offending Him—what must He not do for that same body after it has arrived in the state of eternal Bliss?

15. Could He create a more beautiful World than this one we see, only to destroy it and afterwards make a new one, as if He could have failed in creating the first, and as if He wished to make a more beautiful one to repair His mistake?

25. Must we not then expect another coming to earth of God made Man, other than that which was seen then? Since He must come as a **glorious Bridegroom** <term>glorious Bridegroom</term> to ally Himself with His Creature, and He was only seen [then] as suffering and despised by it? My dear Brother, I wish that you could penetrate these secrets in order to live in expectation of these things, and that your soul might be, through this Hope, the legitimate Bride of this **spotless Lamb** <term>spotless Lamb</term> <note>original: "Agneau sans macule," a reference to the ritual purity of a sacrificial lamb, applied here to the sinless nature of Christ.</note>: for otherwise it would have been better if you had never been created. I require only your attention, unburdened by any other thing, to show you this hidden Treasure of the Coming in glory of Jesus Christ and of the **eternal Covenant** <term>eternal Covenant</term> <note>original: "Alliance eternelle," referring to a final, unbreakable spiritual bond between God and humanity.</note> that He will make with men here on the earth. You should sell all that you have to buy this **precious stone** <note>A reference to the Parable of the Pearl of Great Price in Matthew 13, symbolizing the kingdom of heaven.</note> which is of such great, inestimable value. None will be able to receive it but the humble heart: because God hides His secrets from the great and the wise, and reveals them to the little ones: for such is His will.

### FOREWORD.

**I**t has been more than twelve years, **Dear Reader**, since I was in Amsterdam, where, in discoursing with various people, I perceived that several of them had internal feelings of **Atheism**, doubting Eternity, the Resurrection of men, and even God Himself. This made me resolve to write this great Treatise, of the **Last**

<meta>catchword: Mer-</meta> <note>The catchword "Mi-" in the original French refers to "Misericorde" (Mercy); in this English translation, the next word would be "Mercy," so the catchword is adapted.</note>

<summary>The author argues for a future glorious return of Christ to establish an eternal covenant on earth and introduces a foreword explaining that the book was written to combat atheism encountered in Amsterdam.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the "Foreword" (Arriere-propos) initiated on the previous page. While page 158 ended with a biographical note, this page clarifies the author’s theological stance against atheism and her decision to write accessible, smaller books for the common people rather than academic volumes for the "learned."</meta>

<page-num>159</page-num>
<header>Foreword.</header>
<meta>catchword: than</meta>

->THE MERCY OF GOD,<-

to show clearly that all their doubts were born of an **atheistic** <term>atheistic</term> sentiment: For if there were no God, there would consequently be no eternity, no resurrection, no Angels nor Devils—in short, no spiritual things. Instead, everything would be animalistic <note>original: "brutal," implying the state of a beast lacking a rational or eternal soul.</note> and finite, sustained only by corrupt nature, as many of these ignorant people say and believe.

But as this matter is of great extent, I have not been able to find the time to finish it. However, I have tried to treat these things at various times in my other works of small volume, so that everyone might be enlightened by them. This would not be possible for the poor, who lack the money to buy such large volumes, which are better suited to rich students than to the common people. It is the common people whom I have more inclination to satisfy than the **scholars** <term>scholars</term> <note>original: "sçavans," referring to the formal academics of the 17th century whom the author often criticized for intellectual pride.</note> of this world, who seek curiosity in the reading of mystical things more than the edification of their souls; they seek more to discover some unpolished terms, or something to dispute, than to find the true truth and the knowledge of themselves.

That is why I have never been inclined to write for these "wise men" in their own eyes, who think they know everything when they are truly ignorant, unwilling to learn anything other than what they have found written in their books—often taking the errors of those authors for solid truths and sound sciences. By such means, they can never discover the **Truth of God**. It is not these self-styled Sages that I wish to teach, for I find them incapable of receiving anything other than the preoccupation of their own spirits. Instead, I write for those who hunger and thirst for **Righteousness** <term>Righteousness</term> <note>original: "Justice," used here in the biblical sense of divine uprightness and moral order.</note> and seek only the truth of God.

To such as these, I have no need to write this large volume as I had originally undertaken it, dividing it by chapters and **scholastic order** <term>scholastic order</term> <note>Refers to the rigid, systematic method of academic writing used in universities of the time.</note>. Such a thing only belongs to those who wish to receive praise from men for their beautiful compositions, valuing fine speaking more than...

--- Page 185 ---
<meta>This page concludes the "Foreword" (Arriere-propos) started on page 158. Antoinette Bourignon explains why she rejects the formal, academic structure suggested by contemporary scholars, preferring a repetitive, scattered style that she believes more effectively reaches the reader's soul under divine direction.</meta>

<page-num>160</page-num>
<header>Foreword.</header>
<meta>catchword: than</meta>

than the utility of souls. For this same reason, various well-educated scholars wanted to extract all the subjects from my writings separately and arrange them in order one after another: for example, as I have treated the Merits of Jesus Christ in several of my works, these doctors wanted to gather all this material to compose a specific book from it—and so on with everything, in the **Scholastic** <note>Scholasticism refers to the formal, rigid, and highly structured method of theology and philosophy taught in European universities. Bourignon viewed this academic style as an obstacle to true spiritual understanding.</note> fashion. But God strictly forbade me from doing so, or from consenting to it, since the repetition of a subject received at different times penetrates the reader's understanding more deeply when it is always repeated in different ways and with more solidity.

This is why I prefer to obey God rather than men, and to write in the manner He dictates to me, and not in that which would please men, who would have preferred to see the large volume of <term>GOD’S FINAL MERCY</term> <note>original: "DERNIERE MISERICORDE DE DIEU." This was a significant work Bourignon promised her followers, intended to reveal the final mysteries of the Christian era.</note> rather than seeing it scattered throughout several of my books, as I have already begun to do in *The Light of the World*, *Renewal of the Evangelical Spirit*, *The New Heaven*, *The Touchstone*, and others already printed; as I hope to continue in my following writings, specifically in the *Academy of Learned Theologians*, in order to fully satisfy the promises I had made in this <term>GOD’S FINAL MERCY</term>. Within those works, several secrets of the divine mysteries are and shall be revealed to fulfill the promise that Jesus Christ made before his death, to send his *Holy Spirit who will teach all truth*: which is truly the *Final Mercy of God*; and one should expect no other, nor a new Gospel, since everything was completed by the Gospel that Jesus Christ left us. This must be put into practice, or else one will perish eternally. This is what I assure you,

->Dear Reader,<-

->*She who loves your Soul.*<-

*At Lutzbourg in East Frisia. 1680.*

->ANTOINETTE BOURIGNON.<-

->T H E E N D.<-

<summary>Antoinette Bourignon concludes her foreword by justifying her repetitive writing style as divinely ordained and superior to academic organization, while announcing that her promised work on "God's Final Mercy" is being released across her various publications.</summary>
